Turn on a small but nonzero
J
and assume the filling of
f
electron at each site is fixed to be
\nu_{f}
. Find the optimal solution.

We now describe the second step in detail.
We start by
introducing
the coherent state in a similar manner as spin coherent state of
SU(2)
spin.
We assume that, for each site
\mathbf{R}
, we have a fixed integer number of
f
electrons (
\nu_{f}+4
), which allows us to label each site via its expectation value of chiral
U(4)
moments.
In other words, for a given a state
|\psi_{\mathbf{R}}\rangle
of the
f
-electron at site
\mathbf{R}
, we label it as
|\{\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\}_{\mu,\nu,\xi}\rangle
, where
\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})
is given by

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{\mathbf{R}}|:\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):|\psi_{\mathbf{R}}\rangle=\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})

(S64)

We will
call the states with such labeling coherent states.

S3.2
Trial wavefunction

Using the coherent state defined in the previous section, we now propose the trial wavefunction of the ground state. Again, we fix the filling of
f
to be
\nu_{f}
and the total filling to be
\nu
, and assume uniform charge distribution of
f
.

We first consider the following state of
f
electrons

\displaystyle|\vartheta\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}|\{\theta_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})\}_{\mu\nu\xi}\rangle_{\mathbf{R}}

(S65)

where we simply tensor product the coherent state of each site and we use
\vartheta
(
=\{\{\theta_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})\}_{\mu\nu\xi}\}_{\mathbf{R}}
) to denote the set of
\theta_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})
.
We now act the Hamiltonian on it and calculate:

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle.

(S66)

|\vartheta\rangle
only describes
f
states, so
\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle
is an operator acting on the Hilbert space of
c
electrons.
Each term in
\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle
becomes

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}_{U}|\vartheta\rangle=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}_{V}|\vartheta\rangle=N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}\hat{\nu_{c}}^{2}\text{ \quad(mean-field level)}

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}_{W}|\vartheta\rangle=N_{M}W\nu_{f}\hat{\nu_{c}}

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|\hat{H_{c}}|\vartheta\rangle=\hat{H_{c}}\quad\quad\text{ ($H_{c}$ only contains conduction electron operators)}

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}_{J}|\vartheta\rangle=-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta^{f,\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R}):\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):

(S67)

Here, we rewrite
{\hat{H}}_{J}
in terms of chiral
U(4)
moment.
\theta_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})
is a complex number

but
:\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):
is an operator
.
Since we fix the total filling to be
\nu

and the Hamiltonian without
f
-
c
hybridization preserves
c
-electron particle numbers

, we can replace operator
\hat{\nu}_{c}
with a number
\nu_{c}=\nu-\nu_{f}
. Now,
\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle
can be separated into two parts: a constant term
E_{0}[\nu_{f},\nu]
(which is a function of
\nu_{f}
,
\nu
)
and a one-body
Hamiltonian
\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}_{c}[\vartheta]
of
c
-electron:

\displaystyle\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle=E_{0}[\nu_{f},\nu]+\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]

\displaystyle E_{0}[\nu_{f},\nu]=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]=\hat{H}_{c}-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta^{f,\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R}):\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):

(S68)

We then write
\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}_{c}[\vartheta]
in a more compact form

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},ss^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}\bigg([h_{0}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}+[h_{1}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\bigg)c_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}+\frac{J}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\xi=\pm.|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})

(S69)

h_{0},h_{1}
are understood as a matrix with row index
\mathbf{k}a\eta s
and column index
\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}
. The explicit form of
h_{0},h_{1}
are

\displaystyle[h_{0}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}=\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}H^{(c,\eta)}_{aa^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})

\displaystyle[h_{1}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}=-J\sum_{\xi=\pm,\mathbf{R}}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta^{f,\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R})[\Theta_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}]_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\frac{\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}+\mathbf{q}}

(S70)

h_{0}
describes the noninteracting part of conduction electrons.
h_{1}
describes the contribution from
{\hat{H}}_{J}
. The last term of Eq.
S69
, comes from normal ordering.

To observe the origin of the last term, we note

\displaystyle-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mu\nu,\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-J}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mu\nu,\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\sum_{|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\sum_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\frac{\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}}{2}[c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\alpha\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\delta_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mu\nu,\xi}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})-\frac{1}{4N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{R}}\sum_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\theta^{(f,\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R})\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})-\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}}{N_{M}}\delta_{\mu\nu,00}\sum_{\mathbf{k}<\Lambda_{c}}\theta^{(f,\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R})

(S71)

h_{0}+h_{1}

in Eq.
S69
can be diagonalized. Let
E_{n}
and
[v_{n}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}
be
n
-th eigenvalue and
n
-th eigenvector

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}[h_{0}+h_{1}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s,\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}[v_{n}]_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}=E_{n}[v_{n}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}

(S72)

where we assume
E_{1}\leq E_{2}\leq...
. Then we have

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]=\sum_{n}E_{n}\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\gamma_{n}+\frac{J}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\xi=\pm.|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c}}\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle\gamma_{n}=\sum_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}\bigg([v_{n}]_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}\bigg)^{*}

(S73)

For fixed
\nu_{c}
, the Slater determinant states that minimize the energy of
\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}
are

\displaystyle|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle:=\prod_{n\leq N_{c}}\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}|0\rangle

(S74)

where
\gamma_{n}|0\rangle=0
for all
n
and
N_{c}=(\nu_{c}+8)N_{M}
denotes the total particle numbers of
c
(without normal ordering). Based on the states we constructed

in Eq.
S65
and Eq.
S74

, we define the following trial wavefunction of the ground state:

\displaystyle|\vartheta\rangle|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle.

(S75)

Its energy is

\displaystyle E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle

\displaystyle=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}+\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle.

\displaystyle=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}+\frac{J}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}|\mathbf{k}|<\Lambda_{c},\xi=\pm}\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\sum_{n\leq(\nu_{c}+8)N_{M}}E_{n}\gamma_{n}^{\dagger}\gamma_{n}

(S76)

where
\nu_{c}=\nu-\nu_{f}
. For given
\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu
,
the ground state
will minimize the energy. Remember, the energy contains two terms:
\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle=E_{0}[\nu_{f},\nu]+\hat{H_{c}}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]
. The first term is fixed for given
\nu_{f},\nu
, and the Slater determinant state we choose will always minimize the energy from
\hat{H_{c}}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]
at fixed filling.

In order to find the optimal states at fixed
\nu_{f},\nu
, we need to vary
\vartheta
and find the configuration of
f
states that minimize
E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]
. In principle, this could be efficiently done numerically via simulated annealing and kernel polynomial methods
Weisse et al. 2006
. Here, instead, we tackle this problem via perturbation theory.

S3.3
Perturbation theory

Instead of numerically solving the problem, we could expand the energy function
E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]
in powers of
J
. We note that

\displaystyle E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]

\displaystyle=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}+\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle\,.

For fixed
\nu_{f},\nu
, the ground state minimize
\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle
. Then we find the ground state by calculating the ground state energy of
\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]
at fixed filling
\nu_{c}
. To do so, it is more convenient to first calculate the free energy
at the fixed filing of
c
electrons
\nu_{c}
and at a finite temperature. We then set the temperature to zero, and find the ground state energy. We take the following partition function

\displaystyle Z=\text{Tr}[e^{-\beta\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}_{c}[\vartheta]}P]

(S77)

where
P
is a projection operator
that fix the total filling of
c
electrons to be
\nu_{c}=\nu-\nu_{f}
being an integer.
\beta
is the inverse temperature. For given
\nu_{c}
,
P
is defined as

\displaystyle P=\frac{1}{A_{\nu_{c}}}\prod_{\nu_{c}^{\prime}\in S_{\nu_{c}}}(\hat{\nu_{c}}-\nu_{c}^{\prime})\quad,\quad A_{\nu_{c}}=\prod_{\nu_{c}^{\prime}\in S_{\nu_{c}}}({\nu_{c}}-\nu_{c}^{\prime})

(S78)

where
\hat{\nu}_{c}
is the density operator of
c
electrons (defined in Eq.
S11
) and
S_{\nu_{c}}
denotes the set of all possible fillings of
c
electrons that not equals to
\nu_{c}
.

The free energy is

\displaystyle F=-\frac{1}{\beta}\log(Z)

and the energy is

\displaystyle\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle=E_{H^{\prime\prime}_{c}}=\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}F

We calculate the free energy by performing expansion in
J
.

To do an expansion in powers of
J
,
we separate
\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}_{c}[\vartheta]
into two parts,
J
independent and
J
dependent part.

\displaystyle\hat{H}^{\prime\prime}_{c}[\vartheta]={\hat{H}}_{0}+{\hat{H}}_{1}

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{0}=\hat{H}_{c}

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{1}=-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\theta^{f,\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R}):\hat{\Theta}_{\mu\nu}^{(c\prime\prime,\xi)}(\mathbf{q}):

(S79)

Then we expand the partition functions as

\displaystyle Z=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}Z_{n}

\displaystyle Z_{n}=(-1)^{n}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}...\int_{\tau_{n}-1}^{\beta}d\tau_{n}\text{Tr}\bigg[e^{-(\beta-\tau_{n}){\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-(\tau_{n}-\tau_{n-1}){\hat{H}}_{0}}...e^{-(\tau_{2}-\tau_{1}){\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-\tau_{1}{\hat{H}}_{0}}P\bigg]

(S80)

We now prove Eq.
S80
. For convenience, we introduce an artificial parameter
g=1
. We first perform a Suzuki–Trotter expansion.

\displaystyle Z=\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\text{Tr}[\prod_{n=i}^{N}e^{-\Delta\tau({\hat{H}}_{0}+g{\hat{H}}_{1})}P]

(S81)

where
\Delta\tau=\beta/N
. We next expand partition functions in powers of
g
via a Taylor expansion (
Z=\sum_{n}Z_{n}
). The
n
-th order term is

\displaystyle Z_{n}=

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial g^{n}}Z\bigg|_{g=0}=\frac{(-\Delta\tau)^{n}}{n!}n!\sum_{i_{1}\leq i_{2}...\leq i_{n}}\text{Tr}[\bigg(\prod_{n={i_{n}+1}}^{N}e^{-\Delta\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}\bigg){\hat{H}}_{1}\bigg(\prod_{n=i_{n-1}+1}^{i_{n}}e^{-\Delta\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}\bigg){\hat{H}}_{1}...{\hat{H}}_{1}\bigg(\prod_{n=1}^{i_{1}}e^{-\Delta\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}\bigg)P]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(-\Delta\tau)^{n}\sum_{i_{1}\leq i_{2}...\leq i_{n}}\text{Tr}[e^{-\Delta\tau(N-i_{n+1}){\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-\Delta\tau(i_{n}-i_{n-1}){\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}...{\hat{H}}_{1}\bigg(e^{-\Delta\tau i_{1}{\hat{H}}_{0}}\bigg)P]

where
i_{1},...,i_{n}
denotes the time-slices where the derivative has acted on. Since we have assumed a specific order of
i_{1}\leq i_{2}...\leq i_{n}
, permutation of
i_{1},...,i_{n}
leads to an additional
n!
factor. In the limit of
N\rightarrow\infty
, we reach Eq.
S80
.

Since
{\hat{H}}_{1}
has a coefficient
J
,
Z_{n}\propto J^{n}
. We truncate the expansion to the second order:
Z=Z_{0}+Z_{1}+Z_{2}+o(J^{2})
. The free energy becomes

\displaystyle F=-\frac{1}{\beta}\log[Z_{0}+Z_{1}+Z_{2}+o(J^{2})]=-\frac{1}{\beta}\log[Z_{0}]-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\beta}[\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}]^{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{Z_{2}}{Z_{0}}+o(J^{2})

(S82)

Now we want to compute
Z_{0},Z_{1}/Z_{0},Z_{2}/Z_{0}
. Before moving on, we first introduce the following notation.
For an operator
\hat{O}
, its expectation value with respect to the Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}(={\hat{H}}_{c})
at inverse temperature
\beta
and filling
\nu_{c}=\nu-\nu_{f}
is

\displaystyle\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}:=\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\text{Tr}[e^{-\beta H_{0}}OP].

where
Z_{0}=\text{Tr}\log[e^{-\beta H_{0}}P]
.
The ground state information can be obtained by setting
\beta\rightarrow\infty
.

The operator in the interaction picture
\hat{O}(\tau)
is defined as

\displaystyle\hat{O}(\tau):=e^{\tau H_{0}}\hat{O}e^{-\tau H_{0}}

Now, we calculate
Z_{0},Z_{1}/Z_{0},Z_{2}/Z_{0}

S3.3.1
Z_{0}

Z_{0}=\text{Tr}[e^{-\beta H_{0}}P]
.
This is the partition of the conduction electrons at filling
\nu_{c}
with
J=0
.

S3.3.2
Z_{1}/Z_{0}

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\text{Tr}\bigg[e^{-\beta{\hat{H}}_{0}}[e^{\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}]P\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\text{Tr}\bigg[e^{-\beta{\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau)P\bigg]=-\int_{0}^{\beta}\langle{\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau)\rangle_{0}d\tau

Using the explicit expression of
{\hat{H}}_{1}
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}=J\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\sum_{a,a^{\prime}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}ss^{\prime}}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\frac{\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau):\rangle_{0}d\tau

where
c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)
is defined as
c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}(\tau)=e^{\tau H_{0}}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}e^{-\tau H_{0}}
. Now we want to evaluate

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}}\sum_{a,a^{\prime}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}ss^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau):\rangle_{0}

Due to the space translation symmetry of
H_{0}
, only
\mathbf{q}=0
gives a non-zero contribution.
In addition, for any operator
\hat{O}
,
\langle\hat{O}(\tau)\rangle_{0}
is
\tau
-independent due to the imaginary-time translational symmetry. This is because

\langle\hat{O}(\tau)\rangle=\langle e^{\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}\hat{O}e^{-\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}\rangle_{0}=\text{Tr}[e^{-(\beta-\tau){\hat{H}}_{0}}\hat{O}e^{-\tau{\hat{H}}_{0}}]/Z_{0}=\text{Tr}[e^{-\beta{\hat{H}}_{0}}\hat{O}]/Z_{0}=\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}
.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a,a^{\prime}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}ss^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\beta\langle:\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a,a^{\prime}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta^{\prime}ss^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}\Theta^{\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime}}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}:\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\langle:\hat{\Theta}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{q}=0):\rangle

This term gives the expectation value of chiral
U(4)
moment of
a=3,4
orbitals.

Since the fermion-bilinear Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}
has chiral U(4) symmetry, not all the operators have a non-zero expectation value. We utilize the following statement: Given operator
\hat{O}
, if there exists a chiral U(4) transformation
g
, such that
g\hat{O}g^{-1}=-\hat{O}
, then
\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{O}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}=0
.
We prove that, for
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a\eta s}\Sigma_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{(\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime})}c_{\mathbf{k},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}/(2N_{M})
with
\mu\nu\neq 00
, we can always find such chiral U(4) transformation.

\displaystyle e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{z0}}\quad:\quad{\hat{\Theta}}_{x\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q}),{\hat{\Theta}}_{y\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})\rightarrow-{\hat{\Theta}}_{x\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q}),-{\hat{\Theta}}_{y\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{x0}}\quad:\quad{\hat{\Theta}}_{z\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})\rightarrow-{\hat{\Theta}}_{z\mu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}}\quad:\quad{\hat{\Theta}}_{0x}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q}),{\hat{\Theta}}_{0y}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})\rightarrow-{\hat{\Theta}}_{0x}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q}),-{\hat{\Theta}}_{0y}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{0x}}\quad:\quad{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})\rightarrow-{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q})

(S83)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\langle:\hat{\Theta}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}=0\quad,\quad\mu\nu\neq 00

(S84)

Here, we comment that, in order to prove
\langle O\rangle_{0}=0
, we require the chiral
U(4)
transformation to flip the sign of the operator. Suppose we consider an chiral
U(4)
transformation that changes
\hat{O}
(one components of
U(4)
momentum) to
\hat{O}^{\prime}
(another
U(4)
momentum). Symmetry only indicates
\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}=\langle\hat{O}^{\prime}\rangle_{0}
, but does not indicate the expectation value goes to zero.

We now conclude the only non-zero component is

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\beta\langle:\hat{\Theta}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}_{00}(\mathbf{q}=0):\rangle=\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\beta\sum_{a=3,4}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\xi,s}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}

(S85)

By only including the non-vanishing contributions.

\displaystyle\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}=J\beta\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}

(S86)

S3.3.3
Z_{2}/Z_{0}

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau_{1}\text{Tr}\bigg[e^{-\beta{\hat{H}}_{0}}(e^{\tau_{2}{\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-\tau_{2}{\hat{H}}_{0}})(e^{\tau_{1}{\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}e^{-\tau_{1}{\hat{H}}_{0}})P\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau_{1}\text{Tr}\bigg[e^{-\beta{\hat{H}}_{0}}{\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau_{2}){\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau_{1})P\bigg]

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau_{1}\langle{\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau_{2}){\hat{H}}_{1}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{0}

(S87)

Using the explicit formula of
{\hat{H}}_{1}
(Eq.
S79
)
, we have

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=

\displaystyle J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2},\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}-i\mathbf{q}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\int_{0}^{\tau_{2}}d\tau_{1}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{2})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}

(S88)

We note that

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2}):\rangle_{0}\propto\delta_{\mu,\mu_{2}}\delta_{\nu,\nu_{2}}

(S89)

To prove Eq.
S89
, we first define

\displaystyle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}=:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2}):\,.

(S90)

We now show, for
\mu\nu\neq\mu_{2}\nu_{2}
, there exists an chiral-
U(4)
transformation
g
, such that
g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}=-\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}
, Then
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
. We consider different cases of
\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}
, and show that in the following cases
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
if
\mu\nu\neq\mu_{2}\nu_{2}
.

•

\mu\nu=00,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}\neq 00
or
\mu\nu\neq 00,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}=00
. We take the case of
\mu\nu=00,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}\neq 00
as an example.
:\hat{O}_{\mu\nu=(00)}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q}_{2}):
is invariant under all the chiral U(4) transformation. However,
:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{1}):
would change sign under certain chiral U(4) transformation (as shown in
Eq.
S83
). Therefore, the expectation value of
\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}=:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2}):
goes to zero.

•

\mu\neq\mu_{2}
and
\mu\neq 0,\mu_{2}\neq 0
.
For
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu 0}}
, we have
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

•

\mu\neq\mu_{2}
and
\mu=0,\mu_{2}\neq 0
.
F
or
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{3}0}}
with
\mu_{3}\neq 0,\mu_{3}\neq\mu_{2}
, we find
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

•

\mu\neq\mu_{2}
and
\mu\neq 0,\mu_{2}=0
.

For
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{3}0}}
with
\mu_{3}\neq 0,\mu_{3}\neq\mu
, we find
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

•

\nu\neq\nu_{2}
and
\nu\neq 0
,
\nu_{2}\neq 0
.

For
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{0\nu}}
, we
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

•

\nu\neq\nu_{2}
and
\nu=0
,
\nu_{2}\neq 0
.

For
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{0\nu_{3}}}
with
\nu_{3}\neq 0,\nu_{3}\neq\nu_{2}
, we find
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

•

\nu\neq\nu_{2}
and
\nu\neq 0
,
\nu_{2}=0
.

For
g=e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{0\nu_{3}}}
with
\nu_{3}\neq 0,\nu_{3}\neq\nu
, we find
\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=\langle g\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}g^{-1}\rangle_{0}=-\langle\hat{N}_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\rangle_{0}=0
.

Therefore, the only nonvanishing term comes from the case with
\mu\nu=\mu_{2}\nu_{2}
. Then we can rewrite
Eq.
S88
as

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=

\displaystyle J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}-i\mathbf{q}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle

One can further show the expectation value for all the
\mu\nu
with
\mu\nu\neq 00
are the same. This is because, for a chiral U(4) transformation
g
and operator
\hat{O}
, we have

\displaystyle\langle g^{-1}\hat{O}g\rangle_{0}=\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}

(note that
{\hat{H}}_{0}
has chiral U(4) symmetry). We let
\hat{O}^{\mu\nu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}:=:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):
. We then show
\hat{O}^{\mu\nu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{1},\tau_{2}}
with different
\mu\nu
index are connected by chiral U(4) symmetry (except for
\mu\nu=00
).
This is because
\{\hat{\Theta}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{q},\tau)\}_{\mu\nu}\neq 00
form a irreducible representation of chiral
SU(4)
group. We can find a chiral
SU(4)
transformation
g
, such that
g:\hat{\Theta}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{q},\tau)\rightarrow\hat{\Theta}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}_{0z}(\mathbf{q},\tau)
and then

\displaystyle g:\hat{O}^{\mu\nu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{0z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

(S91)

, where we have pick
\mu\nu=0z
as a representative.

To show it we first introduce the following chiral transformations:

\displaystyle e^{i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{y0}}:\hat{O}^{x\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{z\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\quad,\quad e^{-i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{x0}}:\hat{O}^{y\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{z\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

(S92)

\displaystyle e^{i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{0y}}:\hat{O}^{\mu x}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{\mu z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\quad,\quad e^{-i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{0x}}:\hat{O}^{\mu y}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{\mu z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

(S93)

\displaystyle e^{-i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{xx}/2+i\pi{\hat{\Theta}}_{yy}/2}:\hat{O}^{0z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\quad,\quad e^{-i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{x0}+i\pi/2{\hat{\Theta}}_{xz}}:\hat{O}^{zz}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\rightarrow\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

(S94)

Then

\displaystyle\hat{O}^{x\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}},\hat{O}^{y\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_1}}}\hat{O}^{z\mu}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_2}}}\hat{O}^{zz}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\text{ or }\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_3}}}\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hat{O}^{\mu x}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}},\hat{O}^{\mu y}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_2}}}\hat{O}^{\mu z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_1}}}\hat{O}^{zz}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\text{ or }\hat{O}^{0z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_3}}}\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hat{O}^{0z}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}},\hat{O}^{zz}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\xrightarrow{\text{Eq.~\ref{eq:chiral_3}}}\hat{O}^{z0}_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}

(S95)

We thus prove Eq.
S91
.
Therefore

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}=\langle\ :{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0},\quad\text{whenever }\mu\nu\neq 00

(S96)

(Here we pick
\mu\nu=0z
as a representative.)

Now the expression becomes

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=

\displaystyle J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu\neq 00}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}-i\mathbf{q}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle+J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{q}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}-i\mathbf{q}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

(S97)

Since
\hat{H}_{0}
is also translational invariant, the moment conservation requires
\mathbf{q}=-\mathbf{q}_{2}
. This leads to

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=

\displaystyle J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu\neq 00}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle+J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

(S98)

Then we only need to evaluate the following four-fermion correlation functions:

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

where

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):=\sum_{\mathbf{k},}\sum_{a_{1}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1}):

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):=\sum_{\mathbf{k},}\sum_{a_{1}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}s\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1}):

one is the U(1) charge, the other one is the spin moment along
z
direction of
a=3,4
orbital with index
\xi
, momentum
\mathbf{q}
.

We will evaluate the four-fermion correlation functions using Wick’s theorem. To do so we first introduce single-particle Green’s function

\displaystyle G_{aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},ss^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})=-\langle T_{\tau}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}(\tau_{1})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}

where
T_{\tau}
denotes time-ordering. We now explore the structure of the single-particle Green’s function.

The single-particle Green’s functions are calculated with respect to the ground state of Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}={\hat{H}}_{c}
(We also calculate the single-particle Green’s function with respect to the symmetry broken state in Sec.
S8
and Sec.
S13
). The ground state of non-interacting conduction-electron Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{c}
(Eq.
S2
) always has chiral-
U(4)
symmetry, and thus has
SU(2)
spin and
U(1)
valley symmetries. The corresponding single-particle Green’s function will also have
SU(2)
spin and
U(1)
valley symmetries.

Due to the
SU(2)
spin symmetry,
G_{\mathbf{k},aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},ss^{\prime}}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\propto\delta_{ss^{\prime}}
and
G_{\mathbf{k},aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},\uparrow\uparrow}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})=G_{\mathbf{k},aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},\downarrow\downarrow}(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

Due to the
U(1)
valley symmetry,
G_{\mathbf{k},aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},ss^{\prime}}\propto\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}
.

This leads to a simplified notation of
the
Green’s function:

\displaystyle G_{aa^{\prime},\eta\eta^{\prime},ss^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})=\delta_{\eta\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{ss^{\prime}}G_{aa^{\prime},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

\displaystyle G_{aa^{\prime},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})=-\langle T_{\tau}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}(\tau_{1})c^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta s}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}.

The analytical formula of
the
Green’s function at zero temperature and infinity momentum cutoff is
given in Sec.
S9
.

Now we are in the position to calculate two correlation functions.
The first correlation function

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})::c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

[Use
Wick’s
theorem]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{0}\langle c_{a\eta s\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}

[Rewrite 1st term]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{0}\langle c_{a\eta s\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}

[Rewrite second term via single-particle Green’s function]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\delta_{\eta_{2},\eta}\delta_{s,s_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}

[Further simplification]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

(S99)

The second correlation functions

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}ss_{2}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})::c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

[Use
Wick’s
theorem]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}ss_{2}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{1})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}ss_{2}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{0}\langle c_{a\eta s\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})c^{\dagger}_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}

[Rewrite 1st term]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}ss_{2}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}\langle c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}(\tau_{2})c_{a\eta s,\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{1})\rangle_{0}\langle c_{a\eta s\mathbf{k}}(\tau_{1})c_{a_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q}}^{\dagger}(\tau_{2})\rangle_{0}

[1st term goes to zero
(as shown in Eq.
S84
)
. Rewrite the second term via single-particle Green’s function]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}ss_{2}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})\delta_{\eta_{2},\eta}\delta_{s,s_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}}

[Further simplification]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

(S100)

Comparing two correlation functions (

and Eq.

), we find

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}-\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}=\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta\eta_{2}ss_{2}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

(S101)

We
then define

\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}-\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}=\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\sum_{a_{1},a_{2}=3,4}\sum_{\eta s}\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi_{2},(-1)^{a_{2}-1}\eta}G_{a_{2}a,\eta}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}-\tau_{1})G_{aa_{2},\eta}(\mathbf{k},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})

(S102)

such that

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}=\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})+\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

(S103)

The Fourier transformation of
\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2})
is defined as

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

(S104)

The analytical formulas of
\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})
at zero temperature are derived in Sec.
S10
, Eqs.
S568
,
S569

and are given below

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi)\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\pi^{2}}{A_{MBZ}^{2}}\frac{|v_{\star}\tau|^{2}}{(|v_{\star}\tau|^{2}+r^{2})^{3}}

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,-\xi)\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{\pi^{2}M^{2}}{4A_{MBZ}^{2}|v_{\star}|^{2}}\frac{r^{4}}{\bigg(|v_{\star}\tau|^{2}+r^{2}\bigg)^{3}}

(S105)

where we perform an expansion in powers of
M
and truncate to second orders.

Combining Eq.
S98
and Eq.

,

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=

\displaystyle+J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu=00}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{2}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle+J^{2}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{2}\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Bigg[\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\theta_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}):\rangle_{0}\Bigg]^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}\bigg)^{2}
