\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})+\frac{1}{2}(\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}})^{2}

(S106)

S3.3.4
Free energy:
F

In summary

\displaystyle Z_{0}=\text{Tr}[e^{-\beta H_{0}}P]

\displaystyle Z_{1}/Z_{0}=J\beta\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle Z_{2}/Z_{0}=J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})+\frac{1}{2}(\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}})^{2}

Combining Eqs.
S82
,
S86
and
S106
, we have

\displaystyle F=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}\log[Z_{0}]-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{1}{\beta}[\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}}]^{2}-\frac{1}{\beta}\frac{Z_{2}}{Z_{0}}+o(J^{2})

\displaystyle F=

\displaystyle F_{0}-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}+\frac{1}{2\beta}(\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}})^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})-\frac{1}{2\beta}(\frac{Z_{1}}{Z_{0}})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle F_{0}-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{\beta}J^{2}\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})

where
F_{0}=-\frac{1}{\beta}\log[Z_{0}]
denotes the free energy of the conduction electron system at
J=0
(with Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}={\hat{H}}_{c}
). Finally we set
\beta=\infty
. The energy is

\displaystyle\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}F

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{0}-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}+\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi\xi_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle+o(J^{2})

where
E_{0}=\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}F_{0}
is the energy of conduction electron system with Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}=\hat{H}_{c}
and filling
\nu_{c}
. The RKKY type of interactions are defined as

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}-\frac{1}{\beta}J^{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\tau_{1}}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}-\frac{1}{\beta}J^{2}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}

\displaystyle[\text{Using$\Delta\tau=\tau_{2}-\tau_{2}$ and $\chi(\mathbf{q},\tau,+\beta,\xi,\xi_{2})=\chi(\mathbf{q},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})$}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}-\frac{1}{\beta}J^{2}\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{1}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\Delta\tau\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\Delta\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}+i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{2}}

\displaystyle[\text{Replace $\Delta\tau$ with $\tau$ ]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}-\frac{J^{2}}{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}

(S107)

where
\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})
is defined in Eq.
S102
.

In summary, for a given
\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu
, the energy of the proposed trial wavefunction can be written as

\displaystyle E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]

\displaystyle=\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\langle\vartheta|{\hat{H}}|\vartheta\rangle|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle

\displaystyle=N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}+\langle\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]|\hat{H}_{c}^{\prime\prime}[\vartheta]|\Psi[\vartheta,\nu_{c}]\rangle

\displaystyle\approx N_{M}\frac{U_{1}}{2}\nu_{f}^{2}+N_{M}W\nu_{f}{\nu_{c}}+N_{M}\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}{\nu_{c}}+E_{0}

\displaystyle-J\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}\frac{\theta_{00}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})}{2N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\eta s}\sum_{a=3,4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi\xi_{2},\mu\nu}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})

where
E_{0}
is the ground state energy of one-particle Hamiltonian
\hat{H}_{0}=\hat{H}_{c}

with filling
\nu_{c}
. The expectation value
\langle\rangle_{0}
is taken for the ground state of
\hat{H}_{0}
at filling
\nu_{c}
.

We note that, for both
M=0
or
M\neq 0
, the electrons equally fill two types of fermion(
\xi=\pm
). When
\nu_{c}=0
the filling of each type of fermion is zero. Therefore.

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}\langle:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:\rangle_{0}=0,\quad\text{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}when $\nu_{c}=0$}}

In other words the linear
J
term in
E[\vartheta,\nu_{f},\nu]
vanishes. Therefore, for fixed
integer fillings

\nu_{f}=\nu=0,-1,-2
, the ground state energy is determined by RKKY interactions. We define the energy coming from RKKY interactions as

\displaystyle E_{RKKY}[\vartheta]:=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi\xi_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2})

(S108)

Using the analytical expressions of
\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})
derived in section
S10
, Eqs.
S568
,
S569
and the definition of RKKY interactions (Eq.
S107
, we find the analytical expression of
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi,\xi_{2})
.
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})
at
\nu_{c}=0
, zero temperature
\beta=\infty
and infinity momentum cutoff
\Lambda_{c}=\infty
:

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi,\xi)=-\frac{\pi^{3}}{16A_{MBZ}^{2}|v_{\star}|}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{R}|^{3}}+o(M^{2})

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi,-\xi)=\frac{3\pi^{3}M^{2}}{32A_{MBZ}^{2}|v_{\star}|^{3}|\mathbf{R}|}+o(M^{2})

(S109)

where
A_{MBZ}
is the area of the first moiré Brillouin zone
and we expand the results in powers of
M
and keep the leading-order contributions
.

Here, we comment that a non-zero
M
hybridizes two conduction electrons with opposite
\xi
indices. Therefore, it induces an RKKY interaction between two chiral U(4) moments with opposite
\xi
indices. However, such RKKY interaction still preserves the chiral
U(4)
symmetry but breaks flat
U(4)
symmetry,
as
M\neq 0,v_{\star}^{\prime}=0
preserves the chiral
U(4)
but not flat
U(4)
symmetry.

At
\nu_{f}=\nu=0,-1,-2
, we observe

•

J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi,\xi)
decays as
1/|\mathbf{R}|^{3}
and is always
\leq 0
, in other words, is ferromagnetic.

•

J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi,-\xi)
decays as
1/|\mathbf{R}|
and is always
\geq 0
(antiferromagnetic). In addition, it goes to zero at
M=0
.

•

In the derivation of the above analytical formula, we take the momentum cutoff to be infinity (therefore, it diverges at
r=|\mathbf{R}|=0
. This divergence will be regularized to a finite value once we take a finite cutoff in momentum space
\Lambda_{c}
).

In addition, the energy function in Eq.
S108
can be described by the following effective Hamiltonian

{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi\xi_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):\,.

(S110)

where we simply replace
\theta_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})
with the corresponding operator
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})
.

S3.4
Path integral formula

The effective Hamiltonian
in Eq.
S110
can also be derived by directly integrating out conduction
c
-electrons. The action of the system is

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=S_{f}+S_{c}+S_{J}

\displaystyle S_{f}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)(\partial_{\tau}-\mu)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)+\int_{0}^{\beta}H_{U}(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle S_{c}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)(\partial_{\tau}-\mu)c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)+\int_{0}^{\beta}(H_{c}(\tau)+H_{V}(\tau)+H_{W}(\tau))d\tau

\displaystyle S_{J}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\beta}{\hat{H}}_{J}(\tau)d\tau\,.

(S111)

Here,

f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)
are
\tau
dependent Grassmann numbers.

H_{U}(\tau),H_{c}(\tau),H_{V}(\tau),H_{W}(\tau)
are defined by replacing
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s},c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}
with
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)
in the expressions of
{\hat{H}}_{U},{\hat{H}}_{c},{\hat{H}}_{V},{\hat{H}}_{W}
,
{\hat{H}}_{J}
shown in Eq.
S34
and Eq.
S63
.
\mu
is the chemical potential.

The partition function then reads

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger},f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s}^{\dagger},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s}]e^{-S_{f}+S_{c}+S_{J}}\delta(\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)-4-\nu_{f}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger},f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s}^{\dagger},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s}]e^{-S_{f}+S_{c}+S_{J}}\int D[\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-i\int_{0}^{\beta}\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)[\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)-4-\nu_{f})d\tau}d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger},f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s}^{\dagger},c_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta,s},\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{f}+S_{c}+S_{J}-i\int_{0}^{\beta}\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)[\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)-4-\nu_{f})}

(S112)

where we use the Dirac Delta function
\delta(x)
to fix the filling of
f
-electron and then replace the Dirac-Delta function with a Lagrangian multiplier in the second line.

We combine the Lagrangian multiplier term and
S_{f}
, and then define the following new
S_{f}

\displaystyle S_{f}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)d\tau+i\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\int_{0}^{\beta}\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)[\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau)-4-\nu_{f})]d\tau

(S113)

where we drop the constant contribution from
{\hat{H}}_{U}
and
\mu
(Note that the filling of
f
is fixed). We also comment that the Lagrangian multiplier
\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)
is only introduced to fix the filling of
f
electrons in the path integral formula, and will not be explicitly used in the calculations of this section.

We can also rewrite
H_{W}
as

\displaystyle H_{W}=\int_{0}^{\beta}W\nu_{f}\sum_{\mathbf{k},a,\eta s}:c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}:d\tau

where we have take
W_{a=1,2,3,4}=W
. As for
H_{V}
, we treat this term at the mean-field level:

\displaystyle H_{V}\rightarrow\frac{V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}\sum_{\eta,s,a,\mathbf{k}}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k}a^{\prime}\eta s}-\frac{V_{0}N_{M}}{2\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}^{2}-\frac{V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}8

At the mean-field level, the action of conduction electron only contains fermion bilinear term

\displaystyle S_{c}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)d\tau+\int_{0}^{\beta}\sum_{\eta,s,a,a^{\prime},\mathbf{k}}\bigg(H^{(c,\eta)}_{a,a^{\prime}}+(-\mu+W\nu_{f}+\frac{V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c})\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\bigg)c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)c_{\mathbf{k},a^{\prime}\eta s}(\tau)d\tau

\displaystyle-\frac{V_{0}N_{M}}{2\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c}^{2}-(W\nu_{f}+\frac{V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}}\nu_{c})\sum_{\mathbf{k}}8

(S114)

where the constant term comes from the normal ordering.

Using
S_{f}
and
S_{c}
defined in Eq.
S113
and Eq.
S114
, the partition function is written as

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle\int D[c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)]D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{f}-S_{c}-S_{J}}

We next integrate out conduction
c
-electrons:

\displaystyle Z=

\displaystyle\int D[c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)]D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{f}-S_{c}-S_{J}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{0}\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{f}}\bigg(\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int D[c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)]e^{-S_{J}}e^{-S_{c}}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{0}\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{f}}\langle e^{-S_{J}}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle Z_{0}\int D[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}(\tau),\lambda_{\mathbf{R}}(\tau)]e^{-S_{eff}}

where the expectation value of a given operator
\hat{O}
is defined as

\displaystyle\langle\hat{O}\rangle_{0}=\frac{1}{Z_{0}}\int D[c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau),c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}(\tau)]Oe^{-S_{c}}\,,

and in the path integral formula,
\hat{O}
has been replaced by the corresponding
c
-numers or Grassmann numbers
O
.

Z_{0}=\int D[c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger},c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}]e^{-S_{c}}
and the effective action is defined as

\displaystyle S_{eff}=S_{f}-\log\langle e^{-S_{J}}\rangle_{0}.

Since
S_{J}\propto J
, we expand
S_{eff}
in powers of
J
and truncate to the second order of
J

\displaystyle S_{eff}

\displaystyle=S_{f}-\log\langle e^{-S_{J}}\rangle_{0}=S_{f}-\log\bigg(1-\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}+o(J^{2})\bigg)

\displaystyle=S_{f}+\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg[\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}-(\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0})^{2}\bigg]+o(J^{2})

The first-order term is

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}=-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q},\tau):\rangle_{0}

where

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}^{(f,\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{R},\tau):=\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\eta,\eta^{\prime},s,s^{\prime}}\frac{\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}}{2}\Theta^{(\mu\nu,f)}_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\bigg[f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}\bigg]

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}_{\mu\nu}(\mathbf{q},\tau):=\sum_{a,a^{\prime}\in\{3,4\}}\sum_{\eta,\eta^{\prime},s,s^{\prime}}\frac{\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{a-1}\eta}}{2N_{M}}\Theta^{(\mu\nu,c^{\prime\prime})}_{a\eta s,a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\bigg[c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a\eta s}^{\dagger}(\tau)c_{\mathbf{k},a^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}}\bigg]\,.

Due to the time translational symmetry,
\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}
is time independent. Due to momentum conservation only
\mathbf{q}=\bm{0}
components are finite. Then we have

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}=-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\bm{0},0):\rangle_{0}

As proved

in Eq.
S84
, only
\mu\nu=00
component are finite, we have (
at
\nu_{c}=0
)

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}=-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\bm{0},{0}):\rangle_{0}

For the next order

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}\mathbf{q}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}-i\mathbf{q}_{2}\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{q}_{2},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

As described
around Eq.
S89
, only terms with
\mu\nu=\mu_{2}\nu_{2}
remain finite. Due to momentum conservation, only
\mathbf{q}=-\mathbf{q}_{2}
component produces a non-zero contribution. Then we have

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\mathbf{q},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(-\mathbf{q},\tau_{2}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

We calculate
\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}
and
\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}
at integer
\nu_{f}
and
\nu_{c}=0
.
The first-order term becomes

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}=

\displaystyle-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\bm{0},{0}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}ad\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):\sum_{a=3,4,\eta,s}\frac{1}{2N_{M}}\langle c_{a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}-\frac{1}{2}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-J\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi=\pm}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):0=0

(S115)

where we use the fact that the filling of
c
-electrons in orbital
3,4
are
0
at
\nu_{c}=0
.

The second order term is

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle=J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\tau_{2},-\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

For the same reason as we give in
Eq.
S96
, all the correlators with
\mu\nu\neq 00
are the same
as the one with
\mu\nu=0z

\displaystyle\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\tau_{2},-\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}=\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\tau_{2},-\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0},\quad\mu\nu\neq 00

(S116)

Then we have

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}=

\displaystyle J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu\neq 00}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{0z}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\tau_{2},-\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

\displaystyle+J^{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\langle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi)}(\tau,\mathbf{q})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(c^{\prime\prime},\xi_{2})}(\tau_{2},-\mathbf{q}):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{00}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

Using the expression of
\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})
defined in
Eq.
S104
,
we find

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}=

\displaystyle\frac{J^{2}}{N_{M}}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

(S117)

Combining Eq.
S115
and Eq.
S117
, we find the following effective action

\displaystyle S_{eff}=

\displaystyle\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0}-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\langle S_{J}^{2}\rangle_{0}-(\langle S_{J}\rangle_{0})^{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S_{f}-\frac{1}{2}J^{2}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

(S118)

where
\chi_{c}
is the same correlation function given before in Eq.
S105
.

We next take the low-frequency limit of
\chi_{c}
. We first perform Fourier transformation

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},i\omega_{n},\xi,\xi_{2})=\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})e^{i\omega_{n}\tau}d\tau

(S119)

with
\omega_{n}=2n\pi/\beta
. The inverse Fourier transformation gives

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})=\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{n}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},i\omega_{n},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\omega_{n}\tau}

(S120)

We only keep the
i\omega_{n}=0
contributions and then have

\displaystyle\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau,\xi,\xi_{2})\approx\frac{1}{\beta}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},i\omega_{n}=0,\xi,\xi_{2})=\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\tau^{\prime},\xi,\xi_{2})d\tau^{\prime}

(S121)

Using Eq.
S121
, the second term of the effective action (Eq.
S118
) now becomes

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}J^{2}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau_{1}-\tau_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}J^{2}\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}d\tau^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau\int_{0}^{\beta}d\tau_{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{q},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\mu\nu,\mu_{2}\nu_{2}}\sum_{\xi=\pm,\xi_{2}=\pm}e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau^{\prime},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau)::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle-\frac{J^{2}\beta}{2}\sum_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mu\nu.\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{0}^{\beta}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):d\tau\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{\beta}^{0}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):d\tau_{2}\bigg)

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau^{\prime},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}d\tau^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\beta\sum_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mu\nu,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R},\tau):d\tau\bigg)\bigg(\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2}):d\tau_{2}\bigg)J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})

where we have defined
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})
as

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2})=

\displaystyle-\int_{-\beta/2}^{\beta/2}\frac{J^{2}}{2}\frac{1}{\beta}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\chi_{c}(\mathbf{q},\tau^{\prime},\xi,\xi_{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})}d\tau^{\prime}

Transforming the action to the Hamiltonian, we find the following RKKY interaction terms
which is the same Hamiltonian as we derived in Eq.
S110
for both
M=0,M\neq 0
, at integer filling
\nu=\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu,\xi,\xi_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

(S122)

We can also rewrite the RKKY interaction with flat
U(4)
moment, using the relation we derived in Eq.
S17

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}\sum_{\mu\nu}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}\sum_{\mu\nu\in\{00,0x,0y,0z,z0,zx,zy,zz\}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\xi}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}\sum_{\mu\nu\in\{x0,xx,xy,xz,y0,yx,yy,yz\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-\xi}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

(S123)

We observe that

•

The interactions between two chiral
U(4)
moment with different
\xi
indices are antiferromagnetic.

•

The interactions between two chiral (or two flat)
U(4)
momentum with the same
\xi
indices are ferromagnetic.

•

The interactions between two chiral
U(4)
moments with the opposite
\xi
indices are antiferromagnetic. However, the interactions between two flat
U(4)
moments are antiferromagnetic for the
00,0x,0y,0z,z0,zx,zy,zz
components and are ferromagnetic for the
x0,xx,xy,xz,y0,yx,yy,yz
components.

•

The interactions between two chiral (or flat)
U(4)
moments with opposite
\xi
indices are induced by
M
term and vanish in the flat limit
M=0
.

•

We also notice that

, in both the zero-hybridization model (
\gamma=v_{\star}^{\prime}=0)
and the effective spin model in Eq.
S122
, the density operator of
f
electrons with index
\xi
at each site
\mathbf{R}
,
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})=\sum_{\alpha\eta s}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}:f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}:
, commutes with all the terms in the Hamiltonian and is good quantum number. The average filling of each
\xi
is also a good quantum number:
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})/N_{M}
. Therefore, we can replace
\hat{\nu}^{f}_{\xi}(\mathbf{R})
,
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}
with real numbers
\nu^{f}_{\xi}(\mathbf{R}),\nu_{f}^{\xi}
respectively.

S3.5
Ground state of the zero hybridization model at
M=0
,
\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2

We now discuss the ground state of RKKY Hamiltonian in Eq.
S110

at
M=0
and
\nu_{c}=0,\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2
.
At
M=0
,
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi)=0
.

In this case we only have a ferromagnetic interaction between
U(4)
moments with same
\xi
. The Hamiltonian becomes

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}\bigg|_{M=0}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{1}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi):{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

(S124)

with
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi)\leq 0
.

We next introduce the bond operators

\displaystyle B_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}=\sum_{\alpha,\eta,s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha+1}\eta}

(S125)

where
(B_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}=(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)^{2}
. We note that the
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})
is measured with respect to the charge neutrality point, and at the charge neutrality point, each
\xi
sector has
2

f
-electrons, so we have a
+2
coming with
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})
.
Via bond operators, we find

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu}:{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu,\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime},\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}\frac{1}{4}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha_{2}-1}\eta_{2}}

\displaystyle(f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}}\delta_{\eta,\eta^{\prime}}\delta_{s,s^{\prime}})(f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\alpha_{2},\alpha^{\prime}_{2}}\delta_{\eta_{2},\eta^{\prime}_{2}}\delta_{s_{2},s^{\prime}_{2}})\Theta^{\mu\nu,f}_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\Theta^{\mu\nu,f}_{\alpha_{2}\eta_{2}s_{2},\alpha_{2}^{\prime}\eta_{2}^{\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-\frac{2}{4}\times(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})+4)+\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}4f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1-\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}))+\sum_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}(\delta_{\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime},\alpha\eta s}-f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1-\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}))+(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)+

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}(\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}-f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1-\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}))+(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)

\displaystyle-\sum_{\alpha\eta s,\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha-1}\eta}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}-1}\eta^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-1-\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2}))+(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})+2)-(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}

(S126)

Therefore, we can rewrite the Hamiltonian as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}\bigg|_{M=0}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)\bigg(2+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}(\nu_{f}+4)-\sum_{\xi}(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{RKKY}(\textbf{0},+1,+1)\bigg(2+\nu_{f}+4-\sum_{\xi}(\nu_{f}^{\xi}+2)^{2}\bigg)+\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{1}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)

\displaystyle\bigg(2-\sum_{\xi}(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle const-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1)\sum_{\xi}(\nu_{f}^{\xi}+2)^{2}-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)\sum_{\xi}(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}

where we use the fact that
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)=J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},-1,-1)
and replace
\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})
with
\nu_{f}^{\xi}
.
const
denotes the constant term that only depends on
\nu_{f}
. We next prove that the following state is the ground state of
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}
at
M=0
(with chiral
U(4)
and flat
U(4)
symmetry):

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{\nu_{f}^{+1}+2}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}\prod_{i=\nu_{f}^{+1}+3}^{\nu_{f}+4}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}\bigg)|0\rangle

(S128)

with the filling being

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2:(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,-1)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-1:(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,0)\text{ or }(0,-1)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0:(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(0,0)

(S129)

and the orbital, valley indices satisfying

\displaystyle 1=\eta_{i}(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\quad\text{ for }\quad i=1,...,\nu_{f}^{+}

\displaystyle-1=\eta_{i}(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\quad\text{ for }\quad i=\nu_{f}^{+}+1,...,\nu_{f}+4

(S130)

We note that the filling requirements in Eq.
S129
minimize the second term in Eq.
:

\displaystyle E_{\nu_{f}^{+},\nu_{f}^{-}}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1)\sum_{\xi}(\nu_{f}^{\xi}+2)^{2}

(S131)

We show the values of
E_{\nu_{f}^{+},\nu_{f}^{-}}
at different fillings

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0:

\displaystyle E_{-2,2}=E_{2,-2}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}16J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1),\quad E_{-1,1}=E_{1,-1}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}10J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1),

\displaystyle\quad E_{0,0}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}8J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-1:

\displaystyle E_{-2,1}=E_{1,-2}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}9J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1),\quad E_{0,-1}=E_{-1,0}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}5J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1),

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2:

\displaystyle E_{-2,0}=E_{0,-2}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}4J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1),\quad E_{-1,-1}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}2J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1)

\displaystyle nu_{f}=-3:

\displaystyle E_{-2,-1}=E_{-1,-2}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{RKKY}(0,+1,+1)

(S132)

From Eq.
S132
, we proved that the filling requirements in Eq.
S129
minimize
E_{\nu_{f}^{+},\nu_{f}^{-}}
.

We next prove that, for
\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
,
B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
. We first consider

\displaystyle f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S133)

with
\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
. Clearly,
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}
will move one
f
electron at
\mathbf{R}_{2}
in orbital
\alpha
valley
\eta
spin
s
to site
\mathbf{R}
and same orbital, valley, spin. There are two possibilities,
|\psi_{0}\rangle
has zero electron at
\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s
, then
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
. If
|\psi_{0}\rangle
has one electron at
\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s
, then there must also be one electron at
\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s
(from Eq.
S128
). Consequently,
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
. Thus we conclude
f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
for
\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
. Then we have

\displaystyle B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle=\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\alpha\eta s}\delta_{\xi,(-1)^{\alpha+1}\eta}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0

(S134)

and also
\langle\psi_{0}|B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle
.

Since

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0}|(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0

and
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)\leq 0
.
|\psi_{0}\rangle
minimize energy of the third term in Eq.
S278
:
-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,+1)\sum_{\xi}(B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi})^{\dagger}B_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}
. In summary,
|\psi_{0}\rangle
(Eq.
S128
) is the ground state that minimizes the energy. We mention that the ground states we derived here form a subset (that of zero Chern number) of the ground states of the projected Coulomb model in the chiral-flat limit
Lian et al. 2021
. We mention that in the topological heavy-fermion model, the effect of remote bands is included, whose effect is absent in the projected Coulomb model.

S3.6
Ground state of the zero hybridization model at
M\neq 0,\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2

We now solve the ground state at
M\neq 0
. The Hamiltonian is

{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi\xi_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi_{2}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\,.

(S135)

where
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi)\leq 0
and
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi)\geq 0
.
However, since
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+1,-1)
are induced by the non-zero
M
, and
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi)
is relatively weak comparing to
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi)
. Therefore, we can treat
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi)
perturbatively.

We separate the Hamiltonian into two parts

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}={\hat{H}}_{M=0}+{\hat{H}}_{+-}

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{M=0}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{+-}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})

(S136)

Becaue the Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}
breaks flat
U(4)
symmetry but keeps the chiral
U(4)
symmetry, so we work with chiral
U(4)
moment
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}
. Flat
U(4)
breaking can be observed from Eq.
S123
, where we find
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi)
leads to anisotropic interactions (different
\mu\nu
components have different interaction strength) between flat
U(4)
moments.

{\hat{H}}_{M=0}
has degenerate ground states as defined in Eq.
S128
. We let
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
be the ground states of
{\hat{H}}_{M=0}
. Based on the perturbation theory of degenerate levels, we define the following matrix

\displaystyle[H_{+-}]_{ij}=\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{H}}_{+-}|\psi_{0,j}\rangle\,.

(S137)

and determine the ground state by finding the lowest eigenstate of
{\hat{H}}_{+-}
.

We first note that
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
(given in Eq.
S128
) can be written as a product state

\displaystyle|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

(S138)

where
|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle
is the corresponding state at
\mathbf{R}
and

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,j}|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle=0,\quad\text{when }i\neq j\,.

(S139)

Then, for
(\mathbf{R}_{1}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2})

i\neq j
, we find

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,j}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle\langle\psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle\langle\psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\rangle=0

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,j}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle\langle\psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle=0

(S140)

Combining Eq.
S140
and Eq.
S136
, we have
\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{H}}_{+-}|\psi_{0,j}\rangle=0
for
i\neq j
.

For
i=j
, we find

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle\langle\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle\langle psi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\rangle=0

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle\langle\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle

(S141)

According to Eq.
S16
,
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})
with
\mu\nu\neq 00,0z,zz
will move
f
electron from one
\alpha\eta s
flavor with
(-1)^{\alpha+1}\eta=-\xi
to another
\alpha^{\prime}\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}
flavor
(-1)^{\alpha^{\prime}+1}\eta^{\prime}=-\xi
at the same site. Therefore
\langle\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle=0
because
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})
will change the wavefunction of
|\psi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{1})\rangle
or annihilate it. Similarly,
{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})
with
\mu\nu\neq 00,0z,zz
will either change the configuration of
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
(and leads to a state orthogonal to
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
) or annihilate
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
. Thus

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=0,\quad\mu\nu\notin\{00,0z,zz\}

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{1})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=0,\quad\mu\nu\notin\{00,0z,zz\}

(S142)

Combining Eq.
S140
and Eq.
S142
, the only non-vanishing components of
{\hat{H}}_{+-}
in Eq.
S137
are

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{+-}]_{ij}=\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{H}}_{+-}|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{i,j}\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}\sum_{\mu\nu\in\{00,0z,z0,zz\}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi)\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,\xi}(\mathbf{R}){\hat{\Theta}}_{\mu\nu}^{f,-\xi}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

(S143)

Therefore
[{\hat{H}}_{+-}]_{ij}
is a diagonal matrix.

We next take the state in Eq.
S128
and calculate

\displaystyle E_{+-}=[{\hat{H}}_{+-}]_{ii}\,.

(S144)

The ground states are the states that minimize
E_{+-}
.

We provide the values of
E_{+-}
of the states shown in Eq.
S128
.

At
\nu_{f}=0
, we find the following states have
E_{+-}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,1-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

(S145)

where we have characterized the states with
\{\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}\}
(Eq.
S128
).
At
\nu=0
, the following states have
E_{+-}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)

\displaystyle\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

(S146)

All other states in Eq.
S128
at
\nu=0
have
E_{+-}=0
. Since
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)\geq 0
, Eq.
S146
gives the ground states at
\nu_{f}=0
.

At
\nu_{f}=-1
, we find the following states in Eq.
S128
have energy
E_{+-}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,1-\downarrow\}

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\}

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

\displaystyle\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\}

\displaystyle\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow\}

\displaystyle\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

(S147)

The remaining states in Eq.
S128
at
\nu_{f}=-1
have
E_{+-}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)
. Thus the ground states at
\nu=-1
are the states in Eq.
S147
.

At
\nu_{f}=-2
, we find the following states have
E_{+-}=0

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,1-\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{2-\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow\},\{2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{2-\downarrow\downarrow,2+\downarrow\},\{2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow\}.

(S148)

The remaining states in Eq.
S128
at
\nu_{f}=-2
have
E_{+-}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},+,-)
. Thus the ground states at
\nu_{f}=-2
are the states in Eq.
S148
.

At
\nu_{f}=-3
, all states Eq.
S128
in have the same
E_{+-}

In summary, we find
f
electrons at the same site tend to fill different spin-valley flavors, in order to minimize the energy from
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,-\xi)
. In a more compact form, the following states (from Eq.
S146
,Eq.
S147
,Eq.
S148
) are the ground state at
M\neq 0

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{\nu_{f}^{+}+2}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},1\eta_{i}s_{i}}\prod_{i=\nu_{f}^{+}+3}^{\nu_{f}+4}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},2\eta_{i}s_{i}}\bigg)|0\rangle

(S149)

where
\nu_{f}^{+}
and
\{\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}\}_{i=1,...,\nu_{f}+4}
need to satisfy the Eq.
S130
, Eq.
S129
and

\displaystyle(\eta_{i},s_{i})\neq(\eta_{j},s_{j})\quad\text{ for }\quad i\neq j.

(S150)

We point out that the ground states of
f
-electrons here give rise to the same ground states of the projected Coulomb model in the chiral-nonflat limit
Lian et al. 2021
.

S4
Schrieffer-Wolff transformation

In this section, we treat the model of non-zero hybridization
\gamma\neq 0,v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0
with the Schrieffer-Wolff transformation
Schrieffer and Wolff 1966
. We focus on the
\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2
. At
\nu_{f}=-3
and in the zero-hybridization limit,
\nu_{f}=-3
is close to the transition point as shown in Fig.
S3
. Therefore, a uniform charge distribution of
f
electrons may not be energetically favorable at
\nu_{f}=-3
and the SW transformation could fail at this filling.

S4.1
Hamiltonian

We first separate the Hamiltonian
with non-zero hybridization
into two parts

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}=\hat{H}^{complete}_{0}+\hat{H}_{1}

(S151)

In the heavy-fermion model with non-zero hybridization, we let

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{H}^{complete}_{0}}={\hat{H}}_{c}+{\hat{H}}_{U}+{\hat{H}}_{V}+{\hat{H}}_{W}-\mu\sum_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta,s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}-\mu\sum_{\mathbf{R},a,\eta,s}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}+P_{H}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{H}+{\hat{H}}_{J}}

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{1}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{fc}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}e^{i\mathbf{k}\mathbf{R}}H_{\alpha a}^{(fc,\eta)}(\mathbf{k})f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\alpha\eta s}c_{\mathbf{k}a\eta s}+h.c\quad,\quad H^{(fc,\eta)}(\mathbf{k})=\begin{bmatrix}\gamma\sigma_{0}+v_{\star}^{\prime}(\eta k_{x}\sigma_{x}+k_{y}\sigma_{y})&0_{2\times 2}\end{bmatrix}.

(S152)

where
\mu
is the chemical potential. The projection operator for a given filling of
f
-electrons
\nu_{f}
is defined as

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}P_{L}=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg[\alpha_{\nu_{f}}\prod_{n\neq\nu_{f}+4}\bigg(\sum_{\alpha\eta s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}-n\bigg)\bigg]\quad,\quad P_{H}=\mathbb{I}-P_{L}\,.}

(S153)

The projection operator
P_{L}
will only keep the states with filling of
f
electron being
\nu_{f}
for each site and
\alpha_{\nu_{f}}^{-1}=\prod_{n\neq\nu_{f}+4}(\nu_{f}+4-n)]=(\nu_{f}+4)!(4-\nu_{f})!(-1)^{\nu_{f}}
is the normalization factor ensuring
P_{L}^{2}=P_{L}
.

Here we also introduce the notation of low-energy space and high-energy scape. Low-energy space is defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{L}=\bigg\{|\psi\rangle\bigg|P_{L}|\psi\rangle=|\psi\rangle\bigg\}

For all states in
\mathcal{H}_{L}
, the filling of
f
-electrons is
\nu_{f}
at each site. We define the high-energy space as

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{H}=\bigg\{|\psi\rangle\bigg|P_{L}|\psi\rangle=0\bigg\}

(S154)

which is formed by all states that are not in
\mathcal{H}_{L}
. We use low-energy (high-energy) states to denote the state in low-energy (high-energy) space.

Then the Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{0}^{complete}
maps a low-energy state to a low-energy state
, or maps
a high-energy state to a high-energy state;
{\hat{H}}_{1}
maps a low-energy state to a high-energy state or a high-energy state to a low-energy state
Coleman 2015
. Then after SW transformation, we
are
able to derive an effective Hamiltonian that maps a low-energy state to a low-energy state or a high-energy state to a high-energy state. In other words, we eliminate the off-diagonal term,
{\hat{H}}_{1}
, during the SW transformation
Coleman 2015
.

However, we comment that the original hybridization term
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
can map a high-energy state to either a high-energy state or a low-energy state. To observe this, we act
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
on a state with filling
\nu_{f}+1
. The resulting state can has filling
\nu_{f}-1
or
\nu_{f}+2
(if
\nu_{f}+2\leq 4
). Therefore, it can map a high-energy state with filling
\nu_{f}+1
to another high-energy state with filling
\nu_{f}+2
. The same argument also applies to the state with
\nu_{f}-1
. It violates the requirement of
{\hat{H}}_{1}
that we explained earlier. However

, we could separate
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
into two parts:
{\hat{H}}_{fc}=\bigg(P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{c}P_{H}+P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{c}P_{H}\bigg)+P_{H}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{H}
(Note that
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
will change the filling of
f
electrons by
1
, so
P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}=0
). We treat the first part
P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{c}P_{H}+P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{c}P_{H}
as
{\hat{H}}_{1}
, and put the second part
P_{H}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{H}
into
{\hat{H}}_{0}^{complelte}
. Then we can perform SW transformation by treating
{\hat{H}}_{1}
as a perturbation.

One may wonder what happens if we take
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
as
{\hat{H}}_{1}
and perform SW transformation. In principle, we can find the operator
S
that satisfies
[{\hat{H}}_{0},S]={\hat{H}}_{1}
. However,
[{\hat{H}}_{1},S]
, which appears in the effective Hamiltonian, would contain terms with the form of
f^{\dagger}f^{\dagger}cc
or
ffc^{\dagger}c^{\dagger}
.
f^{\dagger}f^{\dagger}cc
or
ffc^{\dagger}c^{\dagger}
which maps a low-energy state to a high-energy stat. And the effective Hamiltonian after transformation can still map a low-energy state to a high-energy state or a high-energy state to a low-energy state. Therefore, we can not treat
f
as a local moment in the effective Hamiltonian. The such issue will not emerge if we let
{\hat{H}}_{1}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}
. We remark that, in the standard one-orbital Anderson model, we always have
{\hat{H}}_{fc}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}
and it is not necessary to explicitly introduce the projection operator.

In addition, instead of working with
{\hat{H}}_{0}^{complete}
, we first drop both
P_{H}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{H}
and
{\hat{H}}_{J}
terms in the procedure of SW transformation, and define

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{0}={\hat{H}}_{c}+{\hat{H}}_{U}+{\hat{H}}_{V}+{\hat{H}}_{W}-\mu\sum_{\mathbf{R},\alpha,\eta,s}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s}-\mu\sum_{\mathbf{R},a,\eta,s}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}\,.

(S155)

If we keep both terms, it would be complicated to derive the effective Hamiltonian, since it contains
f
-
c
hybridization and
f
-
c
interactions.

S4.1.1
\hat{H}_{0}

We provide the explicit form of
{\hat{H}}_{0}
in this section. We simplify the notation by using single index
m
to label orbital
\alpha
, valley
\eta
and spin
\sigma
and let

f_{\mathbf{R},m}:=f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha\eta s},c_{\mathbf{k},m}:=c_{\mathbf{k},a\eta s}
. The corresponding density operator is defined as

\displaystyle n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m}=f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}\quad,\quad n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}=\sum_{m}n^{f}_{\mathbf{R}m}\quad,\quad N_{f}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},m}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}

\displaystyle N_{c}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}c_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}\,.

(S156)

The kinetic term of conduction electrons now becomes

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{c}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},m,m^{\prime}}H^{c}_{mm^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})c_{\mathbf{k},m}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},m^{\prime}}.

(S157)

where
H^{c}_{mm^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
is the hopping matrix of conduction electrons with new label
m,m^{\prime}
. We next introduce eigenvalues and eigenvectors of
H^{c}_{mm^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\alpha}H^{c}_{mn}(\mathbf{k})U_{\mathbf{k},na}=\epsilon_{k,a}U_{\mathbf{k},ma}\,,

(S158)

and the operator in the band basis

\displaystyle\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}=\sum_{n}U_{\mathbf{k},na}^{*}c_{\mathbf{k},n}\,.

(S159)

Then we have
{\hat{H}}_{c}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}
.

The Hubbard interactions between
f
electrons now become

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{U}=

\displaystyle\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(:\sum_{m}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}:\bigg)^{2}=\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}(\sum_{m}(f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}-1/2))^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m^{\prime},m}n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f}n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}+\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}4^{2}-\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},m}2f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}4

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m^{\prime},m,m^{\prime}\neq m}n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f}n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}+\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},m}n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f}+8UN_{M}-\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},m}8n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime},m,m\neq m^{\prime}}n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m}n^{f}_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}-\frac{7U}{2}N_{f}+8UN_{M}\,.

(S160)

where
N_{M}
is the total number of moiré unit cells.

For the Coulomb interactions between
c
electrons, we only include the
\mathbf{q}=0
contribution:
V(\mathbf{q})=V_{0}\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}
. This leads to

\displaystyle\hat{{\hat{H}}}_{V}=

\displaystyle\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}(\sum_{\mathbf{k},m}:c_{\mathbf{k},m}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},m}:)^{2}=\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\bigg(N_{c}-\sum_{\mathbf{k}}8\bigg)^{2}=\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}N_{M}}N_{c}^{2}-\bigg(\frac{8V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1\bigg)N_{c}+\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}N_{M}}(\sum_{\mathbf{k}}8)^{2}

(S161)

where
\Omega_{0}
is the area of the first moiré Brillouin zone.

The density-density interactions between
f,c
are

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{W}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}:n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}::c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}:e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}=W\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k},a}(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-4)(c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}-\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}}{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}+\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}(-\frac{1}{2})-\frac{4W}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle+\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}(-4)(-\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}}{2})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}-8W\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-4WN_{c}+32W\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1

Here is the derivation,
we only consider the
c
-electrons in the first moiré Brillouin by taking a specific momentum cutoff
\Lambda_{c}
.

Due to the first term in
{\hat{H}}_{W}
(which contains
c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}
),
it is complicated
to find an analytical expression of the SW transformation. However, if we stick to the case, where the fillings of
f
fermion are the same at each site, we can replace
n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}
by its average value
\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}n_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}^{f}
. This leads to

\displaystyle\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}\rightarrow

\displaystyle\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\bigg(\frac{\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}n_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}^{f}}{N_{M}}\bigg)c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}=\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}n_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}^{f}\sum_{a,\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}\delta_{\mathbf{q},0}

\displaystyle=\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}c_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}=\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

Now, we define our new
{\hat{H}}_{W}
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{W}=\frac{W}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},a}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}-8W\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-4WN_{c}+32W\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1

Combining all terms and rearranging them, we have

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{0}=\hat{h}_{U}+\hat{h}_{E_{f}}+\hat{h}_{V_{c}}+\hat{h}_{c}+\hat{h}_{W}+\hat{h}_{const}

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{U}=\frac{U}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m,m^{\prime},m\neq m^{\prime}}n_{\mathbf{R}m}^{f}n_{\mathbf{R}m^{\prime}}^{f}\quad,\quad\hat{h}_{E_{f}}=E_{f}N_{f}

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{V_{c}}=E_{c}N_{c}+V_{c}N_{c}^{2}\quad,\quad\hat{h}_{c}=\sum_{\mathbf{k},a}\epsilon_{k,a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle\hat{h}_{W}=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}c_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},a}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}\quad,\quad\hat{h}_{const}=8UN_{M}+\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}N_{M}}(\sum_{\mathbf{k}}8)^{2}+32W\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1

(S162)

where

\displaystyle E_{f}=-\frac{7U}{2}-\frac{8W}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1-\mu\quad,\quad E_{c}=-4W-\frac{8V_{0}}{\Omega_{0}N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}1-\mu\quad,\quad V_{c}=\frac{V_{0}}{2\Omega_{0}N_{M}}

(S163)

S4.1.2
{\hat{H}}_{1}

{\hat{H}}_{1}
can be written as

\displaystyle\hat{H}_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m,m^{\prime}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\mathbf{R}}H_{mm^{\prime}}^{fc}(\mathbf{k})(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},m^{\prime}}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}c_{\mathbf{k},m^{\prime}}P_{L})+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m,n}e^{i\mathbf{k}\mathbf{R}}(\sum_{m^{\prime}}H_{mm^{\prime}}^{fc}(\mathbf{k})U_{\mathbf{k},m^{\prime}n})(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}P_{L})+\text{h.c.}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{R}}\sum_{m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}P_{L})+\text{h.c.}

(S164)

where
H^{fc}(\mathbf{k})
is the hybridization matrix (with damping factor included, Eq.
S3
, Eq.
S4
) with new label
m,m^{\prime}
.
V_{\mathbf{k},mn}
is defined as
V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{N_{M}}}\sum_{m^{\prime}}e^{i\mathbf{k}\mathbf{R}}H^{fc}_{mm^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k})U_{\mathbf{k},m^{\prime}n}
.

S4.2
Procedure of Schrieffer–Wolff transformation

We now perform SW transformation. We aim to find operator
S
such that

\displaystyle S=-S^{\dagger}\quad,\quad[{\hat{H}}_{0},S]={\hat{H}}_{1}\,.

(S165)

This allows us to introduce the following unitary transformations

\displaystyle e^{S}{\hat{H}}e^{-S}\approx{\hat{H}}_{0}+({\hat{H}}_{1}-[{\hat{H}}_{0},S])+[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]+\frac{1}{2}[[{\hat{H}}_{0},S],S]={\hat{H}}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]\,,

(S166)

and obtain the effective Hamiltonian

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{eff}={\hat{H}}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]

(S167)

S4.3
Expression of
S

In this section, we provide the analytical expression of
S
. We first decompose
S
based on its anti-hermitian property

\displaystyle S=S_{1}-S_{1}^{\dagger}\,.

(S168)

We then assume

\displaystyle S_{1}^{\dagger}=P_{L}s^{\dagger}_{1}+s^{\dagger}_{1}P_{L}

\displaystyle s_{1}^{\dagger}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}

(S169)

where
V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}
is the hybridization matrix between
f
-fermion and
\gamma
-fermion.
R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}
is an
operator
with assumed commutation relations
[R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn},\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},n}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n^{\prime}}]=0
,
[R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn},f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},n^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},n^{\prime}}]=0

and

[R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn},P_{L}]=0
.
R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}
are determined by requiring
[{\hat{H}}_{0},S]={\hat{H}}_{1}
.

To find the expression of
R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},mn}
, we directly calculate
[\hat{H}_{0},s_{1}]
. Before moving to the calculations, we first list several useful commutation relations:

\displaystyle[n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f},f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}]=\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle[\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a^{\prime}},\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},b}]=-\delta_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{a,b}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{q},a^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\text{Given operators }A,B,C,D\text{ with }[A,D]=[B,C]=0,\text{ we have }[AB,CD]=[A,C]DB+CA[B,D]+[A,C][B,D]

(S170)

For each term in
\hat{H}_{0}

(Eq.
S162
), we use Eq.
S170
and find

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{U},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m,m^{\prime},m\neq m^{\prime}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},a}UV_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}n_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}^{f}[n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f},f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}]\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}UV_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\sum_{m^{\prime},m^{\prime}\neq m}n_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}^{f}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}UV_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}(\sum_{m^{\prime}}n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f}-1)f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}UV_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-1)f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}}

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{E_{f}},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}E_{f}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}[n_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f},f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}]\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}E_{f}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{c},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\epsilon_{k^{\prime},a^{\prime}}(-f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}+\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\epsilon_{k^{\prime},a^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}(-\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}-\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a})\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\epsilon_{k,a}(-f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\delta_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{a,a^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}})=-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\epsilon_{k,a}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{V_{c}},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}[V_{c}N_{c}^{2}+E_{c}N_{c},\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}[V_{c}(N_{c}^{2}-(N_{c}+1)^{2})\gamma_{\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a}}-E_{c}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}a}}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}[V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}-E_{c}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}]

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{W},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}R_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}[n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a},f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}R_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}\bigg(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a},\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}-f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}^{\dagger}n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}R_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}^{\dagger}\bigg(\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a},\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}-\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},n}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}\bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{a,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}R_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}^{\dagger}(-\delta_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\delta_{n,a})\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}),mn}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}(-1)f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}

\displaystyle[\hat{h}_{const},s_{1}^{\dagger}]

\displaystyle=0

We notice that
P_{L}
only contains the density operator of
f
electron, and it commutes with
{\hat{H}}_{0}
. Then we have
[{\hat{H}}_{0},S_{1}^{\dagger}]=[{\hat{H}}_{0},P_{L}s_{1}^{\dagger}+s_{1}^{\dagger}P_{L}=P_{L}[{\hat{H}}_{0},s_{1}^{\dagger}]+[{\hat{H}}_{0},s_{1}^{\dagger}]P_{L}
.

Combining all terms, we have

\displaystyle[\hat{H}_{0},S_{1}^{\dagger}]=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\bigg[U(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-1)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}\bigg]\bigg(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}P_{L}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{m,n}V_{\mathbf{R}(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q}),mn}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},mn}e^{-i\mathbf{q}\mathbf{R}}(-1)\bigg(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},n}P_{L}\bigg)

(S171)

From the definition of
P_{L}
(
Eq.
S153
), we note that

\displaystyle n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}P_{L}=(\nu_{f}+4)P_{L}

with
\nu_{f}
the integer number that characterizes the filling of the low-energy state. We let
R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}

\displaystyle R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}=-\frac{1}{U(\nu_{f}+3)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}-W}

(S172)

Combining the definition of
R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}
(Eq.
S172
) and Eq.
S171
, we have

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{0},S_{1}^{\dagger}]=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\frac{U(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-1)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}}{U(\nu_{f}+3)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}-W}\bigg(P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}+f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}P_{L}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N_{M}}W\sum_{\mathbf{q},\mathbf{k}}\sum_{m,n}\frac{1}{U(\nu_{f}+3)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{q},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}-W}

which gives the correct commutation relations

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{0},-S_{1}^{\dagger}]=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{0},S_{1}]=\bigg([{\hat{H}}_{0},-S_{1}^{\dagger}]\bigg)^{\dagger}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a}V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}\,,`

and

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{0},S]=[{\hat{H}}_{0},S_{1}-S_{1}^{\dagger}]={\hat{H}}_{1}

S4.4
Effective Hamiltonian from SW transformation

We now derive the effective Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{eff}={\hat{H}}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]
. Combining Eq.
S168
and Eq.
S169
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{1}{2}(P_{L}s_{1}+s_{1}P_{L}-P_{L}s_{1}^{\dagger}-s_{1}^{\dagger}P_{L})(P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L})-\frac{1}{2}(P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L})(P_{L}s_{1}+s_{1}P_{L}-P_{L}s_{1}^{\dagger}-s_{1}^{\dagger}P_{L})}

Since
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
and
s_{1}
change the filling of
f
by
\pm 1
, both
{\hat{H}}_{fc}
and
s_{1}
map a low-energy to high-energy state, which indicates

\displaystyle P_{L}s_{1}P_{L}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}=0

\displaystyle P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{H}\quad,\quad{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}=P_{H}{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}\quad,\quad P_{L}s_{1}=P_{L}s_{1}P_{H}\quad,\quad s_{1}P_{L}=P_{H}s_{1}P_{L}

Then we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]=-\frac{1}{2}P_{L}\bigg[(s_{1}^{\dagger}-s_{1}){\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}(-s_{1}^{\dagger}+s_{2})\bigg]P_{L}-\frac{1}{2}P_{H}\bigg[(s_{1}^{\dagger}-s_{1})P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{fc}+{\hat{H}}_{fc}P_{L}(s_{1}-s_{1}^{\dagger})\bigg]P_{H}

(S173)

We note that the effective Hamiltonian
{\hat{H}}_{eff}={\hat{H}}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]
will only map a low-energy state to a low-energy state or a high-energy state to a high-energy state. We focus on the effective Hamiltonian that controls the low-energy physics, which is defined in the low-energy Hilbert space:
{\hat{H}}_{eff}=P_{L}{\hat{H}}_{0}P_{L}+\frac{1}{2}P_{L}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]P_{L}
.
Combining Eq.
S173
, Eq.
S172
and Eq.
S164
, we find

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}P_{L}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]P_{L}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}P_{L}\bigg\{\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},m,a,\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\bigg[

\displaystyle\bigg(V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}-V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}^{*}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}R^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\bigg)\bigg(V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}+V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\bigg(V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}+V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}\bigg)\bigg(-V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}+\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}^{*}R^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\bigg)\bigg]

\displaystyle\bigg\}P_{L}

(S174)

Annihilating or creating two electrons would obviously violate the uniform charge distribution of
f
, and will map low-energy space to high energy space. Therefore,

\displaystyle P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}P_{L}=0\quad,\quad P_{L}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m}P_{L}=0

\displaystyle P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}P_{L}\propto\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\quad,\quad P_{L}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}P_{L}\propto\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\,,

and
Eq.
S174
can be written as

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}P_{L}[S,{\hat{H}}_{1}]P_{L}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime},ma,m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}\bigg[P_{L}(V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}\gamma_{k,a}\gamma_{k^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger})P_{L}

\displaystyle-P_{L}(V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}R^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}})P_{L}

\displaystyle+P_{L}(V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}^{*}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k},a}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}^{\dagger})P_{L}-P_{L}(V_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}^{*}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\gamma^{\dagger}_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a})P_{L}\bigg]

(S175)

We next evaluate each term in the above equation (
Eq.
S175
).
The first term
of Eq.
S175
reads

\displaystyle-V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}R_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}\gamma_{k,a}\gamma_{k^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle V^{*}_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k}^{\prime},m^{\prime}a^{\prime}}V_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{k},ma}\frac{1}{U(n_{\mathbf{R}}^{f}-1)+E_{f}-\epsilon_{\mathbf{k},a}+V_{c}(-2N_{c}-1)-E_{c}+W/N_{M}(N_{c}-N_{f}+1)}f_{\mathbf{R},m}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},m^{\prime}}\gamma_{\mathbf{k},a}\gamma_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},a^{\prime}}^{\dagger}
