\displaystyle J^{RKKY}_{0}(\mathbf{R})=\frac{-\pi^{3}}{|v_{\star}|A_{MBZ}^{2}}\bigg[\frac{\gamma^{4}}{16D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}\bigg]\frac{1}{|\mathbf{R}|^{3}}\leq 0

\displaystyle J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{R})=-\frac{\pi^{3}}{8A_{MBZ}^{2}|v_{\star}|}\bigg[\frac{3J\gamma^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}+\frac{J^{2}}{2}\bigg]\frac{1}{|\mathbf{R}|^{3}}\leq 0

\displaystyle J^{RKKY}_{2}(\mathbf{R})=J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R})=J^{RKKY}_{4,\xi}(\mathbf{R})=J^{RKKY}_{5,\xi}(\mathbf{R})=0

(S235)

The RKKY Hamiltonian (Eq.
S230
) now becomes

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R})::\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi^{\prime}\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu,\xi}J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi^{\prime}\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

(S236)

where the first term has
U(8)
symmetry and the second term only has flat
U(4)
symmetry. We then introduce the bond operator

\displaystyle A^{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}=\sum_{i,\xi}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi})^{\dagger}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}

(S237)

where we use the
\psi
basis defined in Eq.
S18
. Using bond operators and the definition of
U(8)
moments (Eq.
S20
), we find the following relation

\displaystyle\sum_{\mu\nu}:\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R})::\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi^{\prime}\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,l,m}\frac{T^{\mu\nu}_{ij}T^{\mu\nu}_{lm}}{4}:\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}::\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},l}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},m}^{f,\xi}:=\sum_{i,j}:\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}::\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}-\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{i,j}}{2})(\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}-\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{i,j}}{2})=\sum_{i,j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}-\sum_{i}\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{2}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}+\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi})+\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}(\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}-\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}})\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}-\sum_{i}\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{2}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}+\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi})+\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}(\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}-\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}})\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}-\sum_{i}\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{2}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}+\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi})+\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{i,j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}+\sum_{i}\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{2}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}-\psi_{f,\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi})+\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi^{\prime}}+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi}+\sum_{i}\frac{\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}}{2}(\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi^{\prime}}-\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{\xi})+\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}

(S238)

Using Eq.
S238
, the first term in RKKY Hamiltonian (Eq.
S240
) can then be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi\xi^{\prime}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R})::\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi^{\prime}\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}+4\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(0)(\nu_{f}+4)+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})(\nu_{f}-\nu_{f})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi^{\prime}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(0)(\nu_{f}+4)^{2}+\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(0)4(\nu_{f}+4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

(S239)

Using Eq.
S238
, the second term in Eq.
S236
can be written as

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi}J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::\Sigma_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)(\nu_{f}^{\xi}+2)^{2}+4\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0)(\nu_{f}+4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\,.

(S240)

The Hamiltonian reads

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\xi^{\prime}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi,\xi}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}

(S241)

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}-}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J^{RKKY}_{1}(0)\bigg[(\nu_{f}^{+1}+2)^{2}+(\nu_{f}^{-1}+2)^{2}\bigg]+{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}E_{0}}

(S242)

where
E_{0}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{0}^{RKKR}(0)(\nu_{f}+4)^{2}+\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(0)4(\nu_{f}+4)+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})
is a constant at fixed
\nu_{f}
.

We now solve the Hamiltonian and find the ground states.
We first note that, for given state
|\psi\rangle
, its energy is

\displaystyle\langle\psi|{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\psi|\sum_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}|\psi\rangle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\psi|A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi,\xi}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}|\psi\rangle

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J^{RKKY}_{1}(0)\bigg[\langle\psi|(\nu_{f}^{+1}+2)^{2}+(\nu_{f}^{-1}+2)^{2}|\psi\rangle\bigg]+E_{0}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0)\bigg[\langle\psi|(\nu_{f}^{+1}+2)^{2}+(\nu_{f}^{-1}+2)^{2}|\psi\rangle\bigg]+E_{0}

(S243)

where the equality holds when
\langle\psi|A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi\rangle=0
for all
\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi
, and we use the fact that
J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\leq 0,J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\leq 0
. At fixed
\nu_{f}
, we then note that

\displaystyle E_{\nu}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0)\bigg[\langle\psi|(\nu_{f}^{+1}+2)^{2}+(\nu_{f}^{-1}+2)^{2}|\psi\rangle\bigg]

(S244)

is minimized by (similar calculations have been provided in Eq.
S132
)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,-1)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-1\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(0,-1),(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,0)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(0,0)\,.

(S245)

We let
E_{\nu}^{min}
denote the minimum value of
E_{\nu}
with
E^{min}_{\nu}=-2N_{M}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0),-5N_{M}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0),-8N_{M}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0)
at
\nu_{f}=-2,-1,0
respectively. Then

\displaystyle\langle\psi|{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}|\psi\rangle\geq E_{\nu}^{min}+E_{0}

(S246)

Therefore,
|\psi\rangle
with energy
E_{\nu}^{min}+E_{0}
must be the ground state. We now prove the following states have energy
E_{\nu}^{min}+E_{0}
and are the ground states:

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(\prod_{n=1}^{\nu_{f}^{+1}+2}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}^{+,{\dagger}}\prod_{n=\nu_{f}^{+1}+3}^{\nu^{f}+4}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}^{-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle\bigg)

(S247)

where
\{i_{n}\}
are chosen arbitrarily and
(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1}=\nu_{f}-\nu_{f}^{+1})
satisfy Eq.
S245
. We first prove
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
with
\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}
. Since
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}=\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{\xi}
will move an electrons at site
\mathbf{R}
with flavor
\xi,i
to another site
\mathbf{R}_{2}
with flavor
\xi,i
, then, for the states in Eq.
S247
, there is either zero electron at
(\mathbf{R},\xi,i)
or one electrons at both
(\mathbf{R},\xi,i),(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,i)
. Therefore,
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi}
must annihilate
|\psi_{0}\rangle
. Thus

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\psi_{0}|\sum_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\psi_{0}|A^{\xi,\xi,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}A^{\xi,\xi}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0\,.

(S248)

In addition, from the constructions of
|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{1}^{RKKY}(0)\bigg[\langle\psi|(\nu_{f}^{+1}+2)^{2}+(\nu_{f}^{-1}+2)^{2}|\psi\rangle\bigg]=E_{\nu}^{min}

(S249)

Combining Eq.
S248
and Eq.
S249
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0}|{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}|\psi_{0}\rangle=E_{\nu}^{min}+E_{0}

(S250)

and
|\psi_{0}\rangle
in Eq.
S247
are the ground states.

Equivalently, we could also rewrite Eq.
S247
with
f
-electron operator (Eq.
S18
)

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{\nu_{f}^{+1}+2}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}\prod_{i={\nu_{f}^{+1}+3}}^{\nu^{f}+4}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}\bigg)|0\rangle

(S251)

where
(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\eta_{i}=1
for
i=1,...,\nu_{f}^{+1}+2
and

(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\eta_{i}=-1
for
i=\nu_{f}^{+1}+3,...,\nu_{f}^{-1}+2
, and
\nu_{f}^{+},\nu_{f}^{-}
satisfy Eq.
S245
.

S6.2
Ground state at
v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0,M=0

We next consider the ground state at
v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0,M=0
. The Hamiltonian now takes the form of

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0,M=0}={\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}+{\hat{H}}_{1}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}}

(S252)

where
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0}
is defined in Eq.
S236
and

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{1}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{2}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{4,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J_{5,\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):\,.

We next treat
{\hat{H}}_{1}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}}
as perturbations and use degenerate perturbation theory to determine the ground states of
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0,M=0}
. We let
\{|\psi_{0,i}\rangle\}
be the ground states defined in Eq.
S251
, and construct the following matrix.
Then we can construct the following matrix

\displaystyle[H_{1}]_{ij}=\langle\psi_{0,i}|{\hat{H}}_{1}^{v_{\star}^{\prime}}|\psi_{0,j}\rangle\,.

We then diagonalize
H_{1}
and the states with the lowest eigenvalues are the grounds states. We first note that
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
(given in Eq.
S251
) can be written as the following product states

\displaystyle|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

(S253)

where
|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle
is the corresponding state at
\mathbf{R}
. From the definition (Eq.
S251
), we find

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R})\rangle=\delta_{i,j}

(S254)

We now evaluate the off-diagonal terms
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ij}
with
i\neq j
.
We first consider the effect of
J_{2,\xi}^{RKKY}
. For
i\neq j

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{2}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,j}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi,\mu\nu}J_{2}^{RKKY}(0)\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R})\rangle\prod_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{2}\neq\mathbf{R}}\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})||\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\mu\nu}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\rangle\prod_{\mathbf{R}_{3},\mathbf{R}_{3}\neq\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{3})||\phi_{0,j}(\mathbf{R}_{3})\rangle=0

(S255)

where we use Eq.
S254
. Similarly, the contributions of
J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY},J_{4,\xi}^{RKKY},J_{5,\xi}^{RKKY}
also vanishes when
i\neq j
. Then
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ij}=0
when
i\neq j
.

We only need to consider the diagonal components
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}
, and the states that minimize
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}
are the ground states.

We first consider the effect of
J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY}

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\mu\nu}J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

\displaystyle\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{2})\rangle\prod_{\mathbf{R}_{3},\mathbf{R}_{3}\neq\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2}}\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{3})||\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R}_{3})\rangle

(S256)

where we use the fact that
J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY}(0)=0
(Eq.
S232
). For the ferromagnetic states (in Eq.
S251
),
\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

does not depend on
\mathbf{R}
. We let

\displaystyle\sigma_{\mu\nu}^{\xi\xi^{\prime}}=\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi^{\prime})}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

(S257)

Then

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\mu\nu}\sigma_{\mu\nu}^{\xi\xi^{\prime}}\sigma_{\mu\nu}^{\xi\xi^{\prime}}J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})=0

(S258)

where we use the fact that
\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J^{RKKY}_{3,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})=0
(Eq.
S232
). Then we find
J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY}
will not contribute to
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}
For the same reason, we also have

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{4,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=0

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{5,\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,i}\rangle=0

(S259)

(Note that, from Eq.
S232
,
J^{RKKY}_{4,\xi}(0)=J^{RKKY}_{5,\xi}(0)=0
and

\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J^{RKKY}_{4,\xi}(\mathbf{R})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J^{RKKY}_{5,\xi}(\mathbf{R})=0
).

Therefore, the only non-zero contributions come from
J_{2}^{RKKY}
(since
J_{2}^{RKKY}(0)\neq 0,\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R})\neq 0
). We find

\displaystyle[H_{1}]_{ii}=

\displaystyle\langle\psi_{0,i}|\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}J^{RKKY}_{2}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2}):{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):|\psi_{0,i}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi,\mu\nu}J_{2}^{RKKY}(0)\langle\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})|:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi)}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,-\xi-\xi)}(\mathbf{R}):|\phi_{0,i}(\mathbf{R})\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R}\neq\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi,\mu\nu}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})\sigma_{\mu\nu}^{\xi\xi}\sigma_{\mu\nu}^{-\xi-\xi}

(S260)

We emphasize that
J_{2}^{RKKY}
plays the rule of picking the true ground state in the nonchiral-flat limit.

We direct evaluate
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}
(Eq.
S260
) and the ground states are the states
|\psi_{0,i}\rangle
with lowest
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}
. We use
\{\alpha\eta s\}
to characterize the states in Eq.
S251
, where
\{\alpha\eta s\}
denote the set of flavors with one
f
-electron per site.

At
\nu_{f}=0
, we find
the following states have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,2+\downarrow,1-\downarrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,2+\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\}.

(S261)

The following states have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,1-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

(S262)

All other states (of Eq.
S251
) at
\nu_{f}=0
have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=0
. Then Eq.
S261
gives the ground state at
\nu_{f}=0
.

At
\nu_{f}=-1
, we find the following states have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=-\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1+\downarrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},

\displaystyle\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2-\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2-\downarrow,2+\downarrow\}

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,2+\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{2-\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2+\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2+\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{2-\downarrow\,2+\downarrow,1-\uparrow\},\{1+\uparrow,2+\downarrow,1-\downarrow\},\{2-\uparrow,2+\downarrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\uparrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\},\{1+\downarrow,1-\uparrow,1-\downarrow\}

(S263)

and all other states at
\nu_{f}=-1
have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})
. Then, Eq.
S263
gives the ground state at
\nu_{f}=-1
.

At
\nu_{f}=-2
, we find the following states have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=2\sum_{\mathbf{R}\mathbf{R}_{2}}J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

\displaystyle\{1+\uparrow,2+\uparrow\},\{1+\downarrow,2+\downarrow\},\{2-\uparrow,1-\uparrow\},\{2-\downarrow,1-\downarrow\}

(S264)

and all other states at
\nu_{f}=-2
have
[{\hat{H}}_{1}]_{ii}=0
. Then, Eq.
S264
gives the ground state at
\nu_{f}=-2
.

In summary, Eq.
S261
, Eq.
S263
, Eq.
S264
give ground states at
\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2
respectively.

In a more compact form, ground states at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
are

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\prod_{i=1}^{(\nu_{f}+4)/2}f_{\mathbf{R},1\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},2\eta_{i}s_{i}}|0\rangle

(S265)

and the ground states at
\nu_{f}=-1
are

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R},\xi\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\prod_{i=1}^{2}f_{\mathbf{R},1\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},2\eta_{i}s_{i}}|0\rangle

(S266)

where
(\eta^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq(\eta_{i},s_{i})
. We also provide the ground states in the
\psi
basis (Eq.
S18
). At
\nu_{f}=0,-2
, the ground states are

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\prod_{n=1}^{(\nu_{f}+4)/2}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}^{f,+,{\dagger}}|0\rangle

(S267)

where
{i_{n}}
are chosen arbitrarily. At
\nu_{f}=-1
, the ground states are

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\psi_{\mathbf{R},j_{n}}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle

(S268)

where
i,j,\xi
are chosen arbitrarily. The ground states given in Eq.
S265
and Eq.
S266
also give rise to the same ground states as derived from projected Coulomb Hamiltonian
Lian et al. 2021
in nonchiral-flat limit.

In terms of the Young tableaux, the ground state at
\nu_{f}=0
corresponds to

2N_{M}

There are
2N_{M}
boxes in the first and second rows.
N_{M}
columns correspond to the
\xi=+1
and
N_{M}
columns correspond to the
\xi=-1
fermions (note that we have
N_{M}
site). The ferromagnetic nature of the ground state indicates the symmetry under the permutation of the position indices. In addition, the ground state in Eq.
S265
is also symmetric under the permutation of
\xi
indices. This in total leads to
2N_{M}=2\times N_{M}
boxes for each row.

Similarly, at
\nu_{f}=-2
, the corresponding Young tableaxu of the ground states are

2N_{M}

There are
2N_{M}
boxes in the first row.
N_{M}
columns correspond to the
\xi=+1
and
N_{M}
columns correspond to the
\xi=-1
fermions (note that we have
N_{M}
site). The ferromagnetic nature of the ground state indicates the symmetry under the permutation of the position indices. In addition, the ground state in Eq.
S265
is also symmetric under the permutation of
\xi
indices. This in total leads to
2N_{M}=2\times N_{M}
boxes for each row.

At
\nu_{f}=-1
, the corresponding Young tableaux of the ground states are

N_{M}
N_{M}

There are
2N_{M}
boxes in the first row and
N_{M}
boxes in the second row. In the case of
\nu_{f}^{+1}=0,\nu_{f}^{-1}=-1
. The first
N_{M}
columns correspond to the
\xi=+1
and
N
columns correspond to the
\xi=-1
fermions. The ferromagnetic nature of the ground state indicates the symmetry under the permutation of the position indices. In addition, the ground state in Eq.
S266
tends to be as symmetric as possible under the permutation of
\xi
indices. Consequently, there are
2N_{M}
boxes in the first row which indicates the symmetric properties between
\xi=+1,\xi=-1
and also between different sites. The remaining
N_{M}
boxes in the second row capture the symmetric feature under the permutation of site indices of the remaining
\xi=+1
fermions.

S6.3
Comparisons of the ground states at different limits

We now compare the ground states at different limits. In general, we find three types of ground states depending on the values of
\gamma,v_{\star}^{\prime},J
.

•

Type I: Defined in Eq.
S128
or Eq.
S251
for
v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M=0
.

\displaystyle\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{\nu_{f}^{+1}+2}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}\prod_{i={\nu_{f}^{+1}+3}}^{\nu^{f}+4}f_{\mathbf{R},\alpha_{i}\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}\bigg)|0\rangle

(S269)

where
(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\eta_{i}=1
for
i=1,...,\nu_{f}^{+1}+2
and

(-1)^{\alpha_{i}+1}\eta_{i}=-1
for
i=\nu_{f}^{+1}+3,...,\nu_{f}^{-1}+2
. and

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,-1)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-1\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(0,-1),(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(-1,0)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0\quad:\quad(\nu_{f}^{+1},\nu_{f}^{-1})=(2,2)

(S270)

•

Type II: Defined in Eq.
S265
and Eq.
S266
for
v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0,M=0
.

At
\nu=0,-2
,

\displaystyle|\psi\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\prod_{i=1}^{(\nu_{f}+4)/2}f_{\mathbf{R},1\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},2\eta_{i}s_{i}}|0\rangle

(S271)

At
\nu_{f}=-1
:

\displaystyle|\psi\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}f_{\mathbf{R},\xi\eta^{\prime}s^{\prime}}^{\dagger}\prod_{i=1}^{2}f_{\mathbf{R},1\eta_{i}s_{i}}^{\dagger}f_{\mathbf{R},2\eta_{i}s_{i}}|0\rangle

(S272)

where
(\eta^{\prime},s^{\prime})\neq(\eta_{i},s_{i})
.

•

Type III: Defined in Eq.
S149
for
v_{\star}^{\prime}=0,M\neq 0
. Type I ground states (Eq.
S269
) with additional requirement
(\eta_{i},s_{i})\neq(\eta_{j},s_{j})\quad\text{ for }\quad i\neq j
.

The symmetry and the ground states at different limits are

Hybridization

\gamma=0,v_{\star}^{\prime}=0

\gamma=0,v_{\star}^{\prime}=0

\gamma\neq 0,v_{\star}^{\prime}=0

\gamma\neq 0,v_{\star}^{\prime}\neq 0

M

M=0

M\neq 0

M=0

M=0

Symmetry

U(4)\times U(4)

Chiral
U(4)

U(4)\times U(4)

Flat
U(4)

Ground states

Type I

Type III

Type I

Type II

For the type I ground states, we have a degenerate ferromagnetic ground state corresponding to
U(4)\times U(4)
symmetry.

For the type II ground states, it is stabilized by a non-zero
v_{\star}^{\prime}
. Due to the ferromagnetic interactions between two flat
U(4)
moments with opposite
\xi
indices, the ground state tends to align two flat
U(4)
moments.

For the type III ground states, it is stabilized by a non-zero
M
in the zero-hybridization limit. Due to the antiferromagnetic interactions between two chiral
U(4)
moments with opposite
\xi
indices, the ground state tends to align two chiral
U(4)
moments.

S7
Fluctuation spectrum of
f
-moments based on RKKY interactions

In this section, we derive the fluctuation spectrum of
f
-moments at
M=0
and
\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2
from the RKKY Hamiltonian in Eq.
S230
. Here, we point out that in this approach, the polarization effect of
c
-electrons has not been included. In Sec.
S8
, we will obtain a more accurate description of the fluctuations of
f
-moments by including the polarization effect of
c
-electrons.

To find the fluctuation spectrum we first rewrite the RKKY Hamiltonian (Eq.
S230
) with bond operators
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}
(Eq.
S237
). Then we take the ground states (Eq.
S265
and Eq.
S266
) and obtain the excitation spectrum of
f
-moments on top of the ground state by calculating the commutator between
\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}
and RKKY Hamiltonian (Eq.
S230
)
Bernevig et al. 2021c
.

S7.1
RKKY Hamiltonian

We first rewrite the RKKY Hamiltonian in Eq.
S230
in a more general formula

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mu\nu}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{1}\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{3}\xi_{4})}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})

(S273)

The new RKKY interactions are connected with RKKY interactions in Eq.
S230
via the following relations

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi\xi\xi\xi)=J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R})+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}),\quad J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi\xi-\xi-\xi)=J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}),\quad

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi-\xi-\xi\xi)=J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}),\quad J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi-\xi\xi-\xi)=J_{5}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi\xi\xi-\xi)=J_{4\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}),\quad J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi\xi-\xi\xi)=J_{3\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R})

(S274)

with all other components of
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})
vanish.

In order to obtain the fluctuation spectrum, we first rewrite all the terms in
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}
(Eq.
S273
) via the bond operator
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}
defined in Eq.
S237
which is also given below (We note that in the previous calculation in Sec
S6
, we only rewrite
J_{0}^{RKKY},J_{1}^{RKKY}
term with bond operators)

\displaystyle A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}=\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{2}}\,.

(S275)

We introduce the following relations

\displaystyle:{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi\xi_{2})}(\mathbf{R})::{\hat{\Sigma}}_{\mu\nu}^{(f,\xi_{3}\xi_{4})}(\mathbf{R}_{2}):=

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j}:\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}::\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{4}}:

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\xi,\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}+\sum_{i,j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{4}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\xi,\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\xi,\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}+4\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi_{4}}-A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{4}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}}

(S276)

Using Eq.
S276
, we rewrite Hamiltonian in Eq.
S273
as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\bigg[-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi_{1}\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{1}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{3})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{4}}4J_{RKKY}(0,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{2}\xi_{4})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{4}\xi_{4}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}}

(S277)

where we drop the constant contribution and use the fact that
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi}=\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})+2
.

We next simplify the RKKY Hamiltonian in Eq.
S277
. From Eq.
S274
and Eq.
S232
, we have
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{1})\propto\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}
and
J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{2}\xi_{4})\propto\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}
. We separate then Hamiltonian in Eq.
S277
into two parts and have

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}+{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1}

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi}f_{1}(\xi)\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi_{1}\neq\xi_{2}}f_{2}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2})A^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}+\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}f_{3}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2})\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\\
(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1})\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})\\
(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{3})\neq(\mathbf{R},\xi_{4})\end{subarray}}J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}

(S278)

where we have dropped the constant contribution and define

\displaystyle f_{1}(\xi)=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\xi_{1}\xi_{1}\xi\xi)+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{3}}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi\xi\xi_{3}\xi_{3})+2\sum_{\xi_{2}}(J_{RKKY}(0,\xi\xi_{2}\xi_{2}\xi)-J_{RKKY}(0,\xi_{2}\xi\xi\xi_{2}))

\displaystyle f_{2}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2})=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R},\xi\xi\xi_{1}\xi_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi}J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi\xi)+4\sum_{\xi}J_{RKKY}(0,\xi_{1}\xi\xi\xi_{2})

\displaystyle f_{3}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2})=-J_{RKKY}(0,\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{2}\xi_{1})

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})=-J_{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{4}\xi_{3}\xi_{2})

(S279)

Combining Eq.
S279
and the definition of RKKY interaction (Eq.
S274
), we find

\displaystyle f_{1}(\xi)=0

\displaystyle f_{2}(\xi-\xi)=0\quad,\quad\text{($f_{2}(\xi_{1}\xi_{2})$ is only defined for $\xi_{1}\neq\xi_{2}$)}\quad,\quad

\displaystyle f_{3}(\xi\xi)=-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)\quad,\quad f_{3}(\xi-\xi)=-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)

Then,
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}
(Eq.
S278
) can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}=-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu}_{f}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu}_{f}(\mathbf{R})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi}\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})

(S280)

Since we fix the filling of
f
at each site, we can replace
\hat{\nu}_{f}(\mathbf{R})
with an integer number
\nu_{f}
. The first term is only a constant and it is sufficient to only keep the second term:

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}=-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi}\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})

(S281)

We next go to momentum space. We consider the Fourier transformation of RKKY interaction
J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}J(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})e^{-i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}
. Using Eq.
S274
and Eq.
S279
, we find

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi\xi\xi\xi)=-(J^{RKKY}_{0}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)+J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0))

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi\xi,-\xi-\xi)=-(J^{RKKY}_{0}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0))

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi-\xi-\xi\xi)=-(J^{RKKY}_{2}(\mathbf{q}))

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi\xi-\xi\xi)=-J_{3,\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi-\xi\xi\xi)=-J_{4,\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi-\xi\xi-\xi)=-J_{5,\xi}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})

(S282)

In summary, combining Eq.
S281
and Eq.
S278
, the final RKKY Hamiltonian in terms of the bond operators can be written as

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}=

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}+{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1}

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}=

\displaystyle-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi}\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu}^{\xi}_{f}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}\\
(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1})\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})\\
(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{3})\neq(\mathbf{R},\xi_{4})\end{subarray}}J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}\xi_{3}\xi_{4})A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}

(S283)

where the constant term in
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}
has been dropped and the RKKY interactions are given in Eq.
S282
. We next use Eq.
S283
to calculate the fluctuation of
f
-moments on top of the ground states at
M=0,\nu=\nu_{f}=0,-1,-2
given in Eq.
S265
and Eq.
S266
.

S7.2
Excitation spectrum from RKKY interaction at
\nu_{f}=0,-2

We take the ground states
|\psi_{0}\rangle
at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
that are given in Eq.
S265
and are also shown below

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\prod_{n=1}^{(\nu_{f}+4)/2}\psi^{f,+,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{n}}^{f,-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle

(S284)

where
i_{n}
are chosen arbitrarily.
We then have

\displaystyle A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0\quad,\quad(\mathbf{R},\xi)\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})

(S285)

We now prove Eq.
S285
.

A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}=\sum_{i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{2},{\dagger}}
describes the procedure of moving one
f
-electron from
(R_{2},\xi_{2},i)
to
(\mathbf{R},\xi,i)
with
(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})\neq(\mathbf{R},\xi)
. However, for the ground states in Eq.
S284
,
(\mathbf{R},\xi,i)
and
(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2},i)
are both filled with either one
f
-electron or zero
f
-electron, because they have same valley-spin flavor
i
. Thus the procedure described by
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}
is forbidden and as a consequence
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
when
(\mathbf{R},\xi)\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})
.
This feature allows us to derive the excitation of RKKY Hamiltonian exactly by calculating the commutator between Hamiltonian and fermion bilinear operators
Bernevig et al. 2021c
.

We first introduce the following commutation relations

\displaystyle[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}]=-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{2}}\quad,\quad[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}]=\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}

(S286)

We then calculate the commutator

\displaystyle[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{f,\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}

(S287)

Using Eq.
S285
, Eq.
S286
and Eq.
S287
, for
(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1})\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})
and
(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{3})\neq(\mathbf{R},\xi_{4})
, we find

\displaystyle[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}]\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}|\psi_{0}\rangle+\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{4},\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}|\psi_{0}\rangle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{4},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{4}}|\psi_{0}\rangle+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{f,\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{\xi_{2}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S288)

We next consider the charge-0 excitation created by the following operators
Bernevig et al. 2021c
:

\displaystyle O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

(S289)

with
u_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})
a complex number.
We comment that, we could also introduce bosonic mode created by
\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i_{2}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
with
\mathbf{R}_{2}\neq\mathbf{R}
. However, the uniform charge distribution of
f
will be violated after acting
\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i_{2}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
on the ground state.
To find the excitation spectrum, we calculate
[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY},O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0},O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle+[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1},O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle
; (
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY}
and
|\psi_{0}\rangle
given in Eq.
S283
and Eq.
S285
respectively).

We first consider
[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1},O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle
. Using Eq.
S283
, Eq.
S288
and Eq.
S289
, we have

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1},O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{4},\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{4},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}\bigg]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\bigg[J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi_{3})u_{ii_{2},\xi_{3}\xi_{2}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}+J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2},\xi_{4},\xi^{\prime})u_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{4}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle-J(-\mathbf{q},\xi_{4},\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{3})u_{ii_{2},\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}-J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{2})u_{ii_{2},\xi_{3}\xi_{4}}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}\bigg]e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}

(S290)

As for the
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}
(Eq.
S283
), we first introduce the following commutation relation

\displaystyle[\sum_{\xi_{1}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2},\xi_{1}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}[\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},i,i_{2},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}[\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},i,i_{2},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}2(1-\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}})|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S291)

where we use the fact that

\displaystyle\hat{\nu}_{f}^{\xi}(\mathbf{R})|\psi_{0}\rangle=\nu_{f}/2|\psi_{0}\rangle

with
\nu_{f}=0,-2
.

Combining Eq.
S283
, Eq.
S290
and Eq.
S291
, we have
we have

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY},O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=\sum_{\mathbf{k},\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S292)

with

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi}\bigg[J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi,\xi,\xi_{1}^{\prime})\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}+J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi,\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi)\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\bigg[J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{2})+J(-\mathbf{q},\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime})\bigg]-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\delta_{\xi_{1},-\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}

(S293)

Here we comment that

M(\mathbf{q})
matrix takes the same form at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
. However, the filling
\nu_{f}
will affect the choice of
ii_{2}
(because not all choice of
ii_{2}
in
u_{ii_{2},\xi\xi_{2}}
produces valid fluctuations) and the values of RKKY interactions
J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})

Using Eq.
S319
, dispersion of
f
-moment fluctuation (
E_{\mathbf{q}}^{I}
) and the corresponding wavefunctions (
u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q})
) can be derived by solving the following eigen-equations

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})=E_{\mathbf{q}}^{I}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q})

(S294)

where
M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
can be treated as
4\times 4
matrix with row and column indices
++,--,+-,-+
.
More explicitly,
M(\mathbf{q})
can be written as

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,++}=

\displaystyle-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle-2J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{1}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,--}=

\displaystyle-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle-2J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{1}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,+-}=

\displaystyle J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,-+}=

\displaystyle J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,-+}=

\displaystyle J_{5,+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{5,+}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,+-}=J_{5,-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{5,-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,--}=

\displaystyle J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{2}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{--,++}=J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{2}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,+-}=

\displaystyle-J_{3,+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,++}=-J_{4,+}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,-+}=

\displaystyle-J_{4,+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,++}=-J_{3,+}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,+-}=

\displaystyle-J_{4,-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,--}=-J_{3,-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,-+}=

\displaystyle-J_{3,-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,--}=-J_{4,-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

(S295)

We define the following functions

\displaystyle h_{0}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J^{RKKY}_{1}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)

\displaystyle-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle h_{1}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{0}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})-2J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle h_{2}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})+J_{2}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle h_{3}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle-J_{3,+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle h_{4}(\mathbf{q})=

\displaystyle 2J_{5,+}(\mathbf{q})+2J_{5,+}(-\mathbf{q})

(S296)

where
h_{0}(\mathbf{q}),h_{1}(\mathbf{q}),h_{2}(\mathbf{q})
are real numbers
h_{3}(\mathbf{q})
is a complex number
Then we express

M^{I}(\mathbf{q})
in a matrix form

\displaystyle M^{I}(\mathbf{q})=\begin{bmatrix}h_{0}(\mathbf{q})&h_{2}(\mathbf{q})&h_{3}(\mathbf{q})&-h_{3}^{*}(\mathbf{q})\\
h_{2}(\mathbf{q})&h_{0}(\mathbf{q})&-h_{3}(\mathbf{q})&h_{3}^{*}(\mathbf{q})\\
h_{3}^{*}(\mathbf{q})&-h_{3}^{*}(\mathbf{q})&h_{1}(\mathbf{q})&h_{4}(\mathbf{q})\\
-h_{3}(\mathbf{q})&h_{3}(\mathbf{q})&h_{4}^{*}(\mathbf{q})&h_{1}(\mathbf{q})\end{bmatrix}

(S297)

We now discuss the choice of
ii_{2}
of
u_{ii_{2},\xi\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q})
.
For the purpose of discussion, we pick the following ground state given in Eq.
S284
(at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0\quad:\quad|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,-,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},2}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},2}^{f,-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle

(S298)

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2\quad:\quad|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle

(S299)

We first consider the diagonal component.
We first consider the diagonal components
u_{ii,\xi\xi_{2}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})
. From Eq.
S289
and Eq.
S298
, we find the corresponding ”excitation” state is

\displaystyle O_{\mathbf{q},ii}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{R}}u_{ii,\xi\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{R})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{2}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}|\psi_{0}\rangle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R}}u_{ii,\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R})n(\xi,i)|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S300)

where
n(\xi,i)
is the filling of
\xi,i
flavors defined as
\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle=n(\xi,i)|\psi_{0}\rangle
, and we have used the fact that
\psi^{f,+,{\dagger}}_{\mathbf{R},i}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,-}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
(as proved near Eq.
S285
). From Eq.
S300
,
O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle=A|\psi_{0}\rangle
, with
A=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\xi,\mathbf{R}}u_{ii,\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R})n(\xi,i)
which is a complex number. Therefore
O_{\mathbf{q},ii}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle
is the same state (up to a phase and normalization factor) as
|\psi_{0}\rangle
when
A\neq 0
. If
A=0
,
O_{\mathbf{q},ii}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle
vanish. For both
A\neq 0

and
A=0
,
O_{\mathbf{q},ii}^{I}
does not create an excitation state and will not be considered. We next consider the off-diagonal term

\displaystyle O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\xi,\xi_{2},\mathbf{R}}u_{ii,\xi\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{R})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi_{2}}e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S301)

A valid mode should have
O_{\mathbf{q},ii_{2}}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle\neq 0
. Since

\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{\xi_{2}}
in Eq.
S301
describes the procedure of moving one electron from
(\mathbf{R},i_{2},\xi_{2})
to
(\mathbf{R},i_{1},\xi_{1})
. This procedure can only be valid when there are zero electrons at
(\mathbf{R},i,\xi)
and one electron at
(\mathbf{R},i_{2},\xi_{2})
.
We next find the valid procedure at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
corresponding to the ground states given in Eq.
S298
, Eq.
S299
.
From Eq.
S298
and Eq. Eq.
S299
, the valid choices are

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=0\quad:\quad(i_{1},i_{2})\in\{(1,3),(1,4),(2,3),(2,4)\}

\displaystyle\nu_{f}=-2\quad:\quad(i_{1},i_{2})\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(1,4)\}

(S302)

Combining Eq.
S294
and Eq.
S302
, the excitation modes are derived by solving the following equations

\displaystyle\sum_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}M(\mathbf{q})_{(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}),(\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime})}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})=E_{\mathbf{q}}^{I}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi_{1}\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle(i_{1},i_{2})\in\{(1,3),(1,4),(2,3),(2,4)\},\quad\text{for }\nu_{f}=0

\displaystyle(i_{1},i_{2})\in\{(1,2),(1,3),(1,4)\},\quad\text{for }\nu_{f}=-2

(S303)

By diagonalizing
M(\mathbf{q})
(Eq.
S297
), we plot the spin spectrum at
\nu=0,-2
in Fig.
S4
.

We next discuss the Goldstone modes.
At
\mathbf{q}=0
, we have
h_{3}(\mathbf{q}=0)=h_{4}(\mathbf{q}=0)=0
(Eq.
S296
). The eigenvalues of
M(\mathbf{q})
(Eq.
S297
) are

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{q}=0}^{1}=-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{q}=0}^{2}=0

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{q}=0}^{3}=-4J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{q}=0}^{4}=-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}=0)-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)

(S304)

where
E_{\mathbf{q}}^{2}
branch gives a Goldstone mode. However, we have four choices of
i,i_{2}
at
\nu_{f}=0
and three choices of
i,i_{2}
at
\nu_{f}=-2
. Then we find 4 Goldstone modes at
\nu_{f}=0
and three Goldstone modes at
\nu_{f}=-2
.
We plot the spin fluctuations at
\nu_{f}=0,\nu_{f}=-2
as shown in Fig.
S4
.

Figure S4:
Charge 0 excitations at
\nu_{f}=0,-2,-3
.

S7.3
Excitation spectrum from RKKY interaction at
\nu_{f}=-1

We next calculate the excitation spectrum on top of the ground state at
\nu_{f}=-1
(Eq.
S266
). We first point out the differences between
\nu_{f}=0,-2
and
\nu_{f}=-1
.
At
\nu_{f}=0,-2
, all the valley-spin flavors are filled with either zero or two
f
-electrons. However, at
\nu_{f}=-1
, there is one valley-spin flavor filled with two
f
-electron, one valley-spin flavor filled with one
f
-electron and two valley-spin flavors filled with zero
f
-electrons (Eq.
S266
). Thus the condition in Eq.
S285
is not satisfied by the ground state at
\nu_{f}=-1
. In other words, not all bond operators
A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi,\xi_{2}}
(Eq.
S275
) annihilate the ground state at
\nu_{f}=-1
.

To obtain the excitation spectrum at
\nu_{f}=-1
, we first rewrite
[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]
(Eq.
S287
) with normal order.
From Eq.
S287
and Wick’s theorem, we have (consider the case of
(\mathbf{R},\xi_{1})\neq(\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{2})
)

\displaystyle[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]=

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}:\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}:-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}:\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}:

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}:A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{f,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}:-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}:A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}:

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{3},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i)\psi_{f,\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f.\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f.\xi_{2}^{\prime}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}n(\xi_{1},j)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},j)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}(n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},i)-n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i))\bigg[\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\delta_{i,j}C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})

(S305)

where
:O:
represents the normal ordered form of the operator
O
with respect to the ground state
|\psi_{0}\rangle
in Eq.
S266
, and

\displaystyle n(\xi,i)=\langle\psi_{0}|\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{v\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}|\psi_{0}\rangle\quad,\quad 1-n(\xi,i)=\langle\psi_{0}|\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}|\psi_{0}\rangle\,.

(S306)

The constant
C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})
is defined as

\displaystyle C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},i)[-n(\xi_{3},i)+n(\xi_{2},i)]

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}[-n(\xi_{4},i)+n(\xi_{1},i)+n(\xi_{2},i)n(\xi_{4},i)-n(\xi_{2},i)n(\xi_{1},i)]

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}[n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},i)-n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i)]\bigg[\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\sum_{m}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{3},m)+\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\sum_{m}n(\xi_{1},m)\bigg]

We also mention the difference between
\nu_{f}=-1
and
\nu_{f}=0,-2
again. At
\nu_{f}=0,-2
, when acting the commutator
[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]
(Eq.
S305
) on the ground state (Eq.
S284
), the four-fermion normal ordered term vanishes because of Eq.
S285
. However, at
\nu_{f}=-1
, when acting the commutator
[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]
on the ground state (Eq.
S266
), the four-fermion normal ordered term will not vanish, because Eq.
S285
no longer holds at
\nu_{f}=-1
.

At
\nu_{f}=-1
, we approximate the commutator by dropping the four-fermion normal-ordering term

\displaystyle[A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}},\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}]\approx

\displaystyle\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{3},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}n(\xi_{1},j)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}(n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},i)-n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i))\bigg[\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})

(S307)

We now calculate the commutator between
{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1}+{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0}
(Eq.
S283
) and
O_{q,ij}^{I}
(Eq.
S289
). Using Eq.
S307
, we find

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,1},O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u^{I}_{ij,\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{3}}

\displaystyle\bigg[\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{3},j))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{3}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}+\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}n(\xi_{1},j)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{1}^{\prime}}(1-n(\xi_{2},i))\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{4}}n(\xi_{1},j)\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{2},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}_{3},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\delta_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{3}}(n(\xi_{2}^{\prime},i)-n(\xi_{1}^{\prime},i))\bigg(\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}}^{\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}+\delta_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{4}}\delta_{\xi_{3},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}A_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}^{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\delta_{i,j}C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})\bigg]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{q}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{N_{M}}\bigg[

\displaystyle J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})(1-n(\xi_{3},j))u_{ij,\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{f,\mathbf{R},j}^{\xi_{4}}+\sum_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{1})n(\xi_{1},i)\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u_{ij,\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{\xi_{1}^{\prime}}J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{2},\xi_{4})(1-n(\xi_{2},j))u_{ij,\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{1}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}-J(-\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})n(\xi_{4},i)u_{ij,\xi_{4}\xi_{1}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{2},\xi_{4})(1-n(\xi_{2},i))u_{ij,\xi_{4}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle+J(-\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})n(\xi_{1},j)u_{ij,\xi_{4}\xi_{1}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{\xi_{3},{\dagger}}-\sum_{\xi_{1}^{\prime}}J(\mathbf{q},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})(1-n(\xi_{2},i))u_{ij,\xi_{2}\xi_{3}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{4}}

\displaystyle-J(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{1})n(\xi_{1},j)u_{ij,\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{3}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1}^{\prime},{\dagger}}\bigg]

\displaystyle+\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\mathbf{q},i}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}J(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0,\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})}{N_{M}}\delta_{i,j}\bigg[(n(\xi_{1},i)-n(\xi_{2},i))u_{ii,\xi_{2}\xi_{1}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\sum_{m}\psi_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f,\xi_{3},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f,\xi_{4}}

\displaystyle+(n(\xi_{4},i)-n(\xi_{3},i))u_{ii,\xi_{4}\xi_{3}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})\sum_{m}\psi_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},m}^{f,\xi_{2}}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\sum_{\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4}}J(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4})\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime},i,j}u^{I}_{ij,\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}_{3}}\delta_{i,j}

\displaystyle C_{0}(\mathbf{R},\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R}_{3},\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{4},\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime})|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S308)

Using Eq.
S307
, we find

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY,0},O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=

\displaystyle-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)[\sum_{\xi_{1}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2},\xi_{1}}u^{I}_{ii_{2},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}^{\prime}}[\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R}^{\prime},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2},\xi_{1}}u^{I}_{ij,\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}[\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R})\hat{\nu_{f}}^{\xi_{1}}(\mathbf{R}),\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}u^{I}_{ij,\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}2(\nu_{f}^{\xi^{\prime}}-\nu_{f}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}}+1)(1-\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}})\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}]|\psi_{0}\rangle

Before calculating the spectrum with the commutator, we first discuss the choice of
ij
in
O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}
. We take the following ground state (from Eq.
S266
) as an example

\displaystyle|\psi_{0}\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},2}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},1}^{f,-,{\dagger}}|0\rangle\,.

(S310)

For the same reason given below Eq.
S301
, the diagonal components
O_{\mathbf{q},ii}^{I}
will not contribute to the excitation spectrum. For the off-diagonal term
O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}
,
O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}
(when acting on the ground state) will move one electron from valley-spin flavor
j
to valley-spin flavor
i
. Then a valid procedure requires valley-spin flavor
j
to have at least one filled
\xi
-orbital (remember we have
\xi=\pm 1
orbitals at each valley-spin flavor) and valley-spin flavor
i
has one empty
\xi
-orbital. Then only the following choices of
ij
(in
O_{\mathbf{q},ij}
) are valid for the ground state in Eq.
S310

\displaystyle(i,j)\in\{(3,1),(4,1),(3,2),(4,2),(2,1),(2,2)\}

(S311)

We further classify them into four sectors according to whether flavor
i,j
are fully-filled, empty or half-filled

\displaystyle\text{Full-empty sector}:(i,j)\in\{(3,1),(4,1)\}

\displaystyle\text{Full-half sector}:(i,j)=(2,1)

\displaystyle\text{Half-empty sector}:(i,j)\in\{(3,2),(4,2)\}

\displaystyle\text{Half-half sector}:(i,j)=(2,2)

(S313)

We next give the explicit form of
O_{\mathbf{q},ij}^{I}|\psi_{0}\rangle
(from Eq.
S289
)

\displaystyle O^{I}_{\mathbf{q},ij}|\psi_{0}\rangle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i_{2}}^{f,\xi^{\prime}_{2}}u^{I}_{ij,\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S314)

For full-half sector with
(i,j)=(2,1)
.
\xi=+,i=2
flavor has been filled with one electron (Eq.
S310
). The term associated with
u^{I}_{ij,+\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
annihilate the ground state (Eq.
S314
). In other words,

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{R},i=2}^{f,+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j=1}^{f,\xi_{2}^{\prime}}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0

(S315)

Then we can simply set the corresponding
u_{ij,+\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})
to zero

\displaystyle u_{ij,+\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})=0\quad,\quad(i,j)=(2,1)

(S316)

since
u_{ij,+\xi_{2}^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})
does not describe a valid procedure.

For half-empty sector with
(i,j)=(3,2)
or
(i,j)=(4,2)
.
\xi_{2}^{\prime}=-,j=2
is empty. Then
\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{+,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j=2}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}=-}|\psi_{0}\rangle=0
(Eq.
S314
) and we let

\displaystyle u_{ij,-\xi^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})=0\quad,\quad(i,j)\in\{(3,2),(4,2)\}

(S317)

For half-half sector, with
(i,j)=(2,2)
.
\xi_{2}^{\prime}=-,j=2
flavor is empty and
\xi^{\prime}=+,i=2
flavor is filled with one
f
-electron, we then set

\displaystyle u_{ii,\xi\xi}^{I}(\mathbf{q})=0\quad,\quad(i,j)=(2,2),\xi\in\{\pm\}

(S318)

As for full-empty sector, with
(i,j)\in\{(3,1),(4,1)\}
, all components of
u_{ij,\xi\xi^{\prime}}^{I}(\mathbf{q})
can be non-zero.

S7.3.1
Full-empty sector

We now consider the full-empty sector with
(i,j)\in\{(3,1),(4,1)\}
.
Combining Eq.
S308
and Eq.
, we have

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY},O_{ij,\mathbf{q}}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi_{1},\xi_{2}}\sum_{\xi_{1}^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u_{ij,\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})M(\mathbf{q})_{(\xi_{1}\xi_{2}),(\xi_{1}^{\prime}\xi_{2}^{\prime})}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi_{1},{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{N_{M}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S319)

where

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,++}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)]-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,--}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)]-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,--}=

\displaystyle-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})(-1)\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{--,++}=-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})(-1)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,+-}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)]

\displaystyle-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,-+}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)]

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,+-}=

\displaystyle J_{3+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,++}=J_{4+}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{++,-+}=

\displaystyle J_{4+}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,++}=J_{3+}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,+-}=

\displaystyle J_{4-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,--}=J_{3-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{--,-+}=

\displaystyle J_{3-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(\mathbf{q})_{-+,--}=J_{4-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+-,-+}=

\displaystyle J_{5,+}(\mathbf{q})+J_{5,+}(-\mathbf{q})\quad,\quad M(ij,\mathbf{q})_{-+,+-}=J_{5,+}^{*}(\mathbf{q})+J_{5,+}^{*}(\mathbf{q})

(S320)

There are four modes for each choice of
(i,j)
, and eight modes in total.

At
\mathbf{q}=0
, we can derive the analytical expressions of four eigenvalues of
M(\mathbf{q}=0)
:

\displaystyle E^{1}_{\mathbf{q}=0}=0\quad,\quad E^{2}_{\mathbf{q}=0}=-4J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)

\displaystyle E^{3}_{\mathbf{q}=0}=-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)-2J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{R}_{2}=0)\quad,\quad E^{4}_{\mathbf{q}=0}=-2J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)

(S321)

where
E^{1}(\mathbf{q})
corresponds to Goldstone modes.

S7.3.2
Full-half sector

We consider the full-half sector with
(i,j)=(2,1)
. Combining Eq.
S308
, Eq.
and also the constraints
S318
, we construct the following eigenequations with eigenvalue
E_{\mathbf{q}}^{I}

\displaystyle[{\hat{H}}_{RKKY},O_{21,\mathbf{q}}^{I}]|\psi_{0}\rangle=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}u^{I}_{21,-\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},2}^{f,-,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{N_{M}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{q}}^{I}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}u^{I}_{21,-\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q})\psi_{\mathbf{R},2}^{f,-,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi_{2}}\frac{e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}}{N_{M}}|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S322)

where the
2\times 2
matrix
M(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
is defined as

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{-,-}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}_{2}=0)]

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+,+}=

\displaystyle 2[J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{0}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)+J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})-J_{1}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)-J_{2}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q}=0)]

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{-,+}=

\displaystyle J_{3,-}^{RKKY}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle M(\mathbf{q})_{+,-}=

\displaystyle J_{4,-}^{RKKY}(-\mathbf{q})

(S323)

Equivalently, Eq.
