\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\bigg[\frac{2\gamma^{2}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}^{*}-2J\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}^{*}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}(k_{x}-i\xi k_{y})\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},-\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}^{*}+\frac{2\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)|^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},-\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}^{*}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\bigg[\frac{2\gamma^{2}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}-2J\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}(k_{x}-i\xi k_{y})\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},-\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}+\frac{2\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)|^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})\langle:\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},-\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi}(\tau):\rangle_{0}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{i\xi}

(S401)

As for
\chi^{A}
in Eq.
S397
, we find

\displaystyle[\chi^{A}_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i,j)]^{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\sum_{\mathbf{k},\mathbf{k}^{\prime}}\frac{\delta_{\mathbf{k}^{\prime}-\mathbf{k},\mathbf{q}}}{N_{M}}\bigg\{\frac{2\gamma^{4}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)F(|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma^{3}v_{\star}^{\prime}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)F(|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}\bigg[(k_{x}-i\xi_{2}^{\prime}k_{y})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)+(k_{x}^{\prime}+i\xi_{2}k_{y}’)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle+(k_{x}^{\prime}-i\xi^{\prime}k_{y}^{\prime})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)+(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}[F(|\mathbf{k}|)F(|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|)]^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}\bigg[(k_{x}^{\prime}-i\xi^{\prime}k_{y}^{\prime})(k_{x}-i\xi_{2}^{\prime}k_{y})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle+(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})(k_{x}^{\prime}+i\xi_{2}k_{y}^{\prime})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)+(k_{x}^{\prime}-i\xi^{\prime}k_{y})(k_{x}^{\prime}+i\xi_{2}k_{y}^{\prime})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle+(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})(k_{x}-i\xi_{2}^{\prime}k_{y})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]+2J^{2}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle-\frac{4\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}}JF(|\mathbf{k}|)F(|\mathbf{k}^{\prime}|)}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\bigg[\gamma^{2}g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g^{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi,i}_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)

\displaystyle+\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}(k_{x}^{\prime}-i\xi^{\prime}k_{y}^{\prime})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{-\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)+\gamma v_{\star}^{\prime}(k_{x}+i\xi k_{y})g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi^{\prime}\xi_{2},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau)g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}-\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]\bigg\}d\tau

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}^{A}(-\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,j,i)

(S402)

Combining Eq.
S398
, Eq.
S401
and Eq.
S402
, we have

\displaystyle[F_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}^{ij}(\mathbf{q})]^{*}=\frac{(N_{i\xi}-N_{j\xi^{\prime}})^{*}}{2}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}-\frac{1}{4}\bigg([\chi^{A}_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i,j)]^{*}+[\chi^{A}_{\xi^{\prime}\xi,\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(-\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,j,i)]^{*}\bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{N_{i\xi}-N_{j\xi^{\prime}}}{2}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}-\frac{1}{4}\bigg([\chi^{A}_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}(-\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,j,i)]+[\chi^{A}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\xi}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i,j)]\bigg)=F_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}^{ij}(\mathbf{q})

(S403)

Thus we proved Eq.
S400
.

S8.8
Flat
U(4)
symmetry and Noether’s theorem

We consider the model at
M=0
, which has a flat
U(4)
symmetry. We note that
U(4)=SU(4)\times U(1)_{c}
with
U(1)_{c}
a
U(1)
charge symmetry. We focus on the
SU(4)
part, which will produce the Goldstone modes (note that the ground states (Eq.
S335
) preserve
U(1)_{c}
symmetry). And we will call it flat
SU(4)
symmetry.

We first introduce flat
SU(4)
transformation. We consider a flat
SU(4)
transformation characterize by real number
v_{\mu\nu}
with
\mu,\nu\in\{0,x,y,z\}
and
\mu\nu\neq 00
. The
SU(4)
transformation on the
f
electron is then defined as

\displaystyle g=e^{-i\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi,i,j}\phi_{ij}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi}}

(S404)

where
\phi=\sum_{\mu\nu\neq 00}v_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}
is a
4\times 4
matrix and
T^{\mu\nu}
are the generators of flat
U(4)
group (Eq.
S20
). In addition,
\phi
is a
4\times 4
traceless Hermitian matrix. We next discuss how
u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R})
transform under flat
SU(4)
transformation. We act
SU(4)
transformation on the
|u\rangle
state (using Eq.
S333
,Eq.
S334
and Eq.
S337
)

\displaystyle g|u\rangle=e^{-i\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi,i,j}\phi_{ij}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi}}|u\rangle=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}g_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{R}[u(\mathbf{R})]|\psi_{0}\rangle

(S405)

where we define

\displaystyle g_{\mathbf{R}}=e^{-i\sum_{\xi,i,j}\phi_{ij}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi}}\,.

(S406)

such that
g=\prod_{\mathbf{R}}g_{\mathbf{R}}
(note that
\phi_{ij}
is site
R
-independent).
Since
g_{\mathbf{R}}
generate a
SU(4)
transformation,
\hat{R}[u(\mathbf{R})]
(Eq.
S337
) generate a
SU(8)
transformation (for each site). Then
g_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{R}[u(\mathbf{R})]
also generate a
SU(8)
transformation. Thus there exists
\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})
, such that

\displaystyle g_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{R}[u(\mathbf{R})]=\hat{R}[\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})]

(S407)

To find
\tilde{u}
, we can act the transformation on the
\psi^{f}
fermion. From Eq.
S407

\displaystyle\bigg(g_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{R}_{\mathbf{R}}[u(\mathbf{R})]\bigg)\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}\bigg(g_{\mathbf{R}}\hat{R}_{\mathbf{R}}[u(\mathbf{R})]\bigg)^{-1}=\bigg(\hat{R}[\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})]\bigg)\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}\bigg(\hat{R}[\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})]\bigg)^{-1}

(S408)

The left-hand-side (LHS) of Eq.
S408
gives

\displaystyle LHS=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}[e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}

(S409)

where
\Phi
is a
8\times 8
matrix with column and row indices
i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}
and
[\Phi]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}=\phi_{ij}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}
.
The right-hand-side (LHS) of Eq.
S408
gives

\displaystyle RHS=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}[e^{i\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})}]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}

(S410)

Then

\displaystyle LHS=RHS\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}[e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}[e^{i\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})}]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}\Rightarrow\sum_{j\xi^{\prime}}[e^{-i\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})}e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi^{\prime}}=\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle e^{-i\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})}e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}=\mathbb{I}\Rightarrow e^{i\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})}=e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}

(S411)

where
\mathbb{I}
is an
8\times 8
identity matrix. We can now give the expression of
\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})=-i\log\bigg[e^{i\Phi}e^{iu(\mathbf{R})}\bigg]

(S412)

We then take an infinitesimal transformation
g
with
|\phi_{ij}|<<1
. From Eq.
S412
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})\approx

\displaystyle i\log\bigg[(1-i\Phi)e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})}\bigg]=i\log\bigg[e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})}-i\Phi e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u(\mathbf{R})+\int_{0}^{1}\frac{1}{1-t(1-e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})})}[\Phi e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})}]\frac{1}{1-t(1-e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})})}dt

(S413)

where we use the derivative of a matrix logarithm.

From Eq.
S413
, we introduce a tensor
Du
which is a function of
u(\mathbf{R})

\displaystyle Du[u(\mathbf{R})]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}=\sum_{\xi_{2},j_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg[\frac{1}{1-t(1-e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})})}\bigg]_{i\xi,m\xi_{2}}[e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})}]_{n\xi_{2},j_{2}\xi^{\prime}_{2}}\bigg[\frac{1}{1-t(1-e^{-iu(\mathbf{R})})}\bigg]_{j_{2}\xi^{\prime}_{2},j\xi^{\prime}}dt

(S414)

such that Eq.
S413
can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R})=u(\mathbf{R})+\sum_{mn}Du[u(\mathbf{R})]^{mn}\phi_{mn}

(S415)

Therefore
Du[u(\mathbf{R})]
characterize the transformation properties of
u(\mathbf{R})
under flat
U(4)
transformation.

We next discuss the consequence of infinitesimal symmetry transformation. We note that we have integrated out conduction
c
-electron fields and derived an effective action of
u
fields by performing expansion to second order in
u
. Here, we let
S[u]
denote the effective action obtained by integrating out conduction
c
electrons and performing expansion to the infinity order. Thus,
S[u]
is an exact theory and has flat
U(4)
symmetry (Here we comment that the effective action we derived in Eq.
S390
describes the fluctuations around one symmetry breaking state and thus does not have the flat
U(4)
symmetry. However, by performing expansion to infinity order, we effectively sum over the contributions from all the possible symmetry-breaking states and the symmetry is recovered.) Then it must be invariant under a symmetry transformation. In other words

\displaystyle S[\tilde{u}]=S[u]

(S416)

where
u=\{{u}(\mathbf{R},\tau)\}
denotes the configuration of the original
u
fields
where
\tilde{u}=\{\tilde{u}(\mathbf{R},\tau)\}
denotes the configuration of fields after acting
SU(4)
transformation
g
on each time slice.
\tau
is the imaginary time. Written explicitly, for an infinitesimal flat
SU(4)
transformation, we have

\displaystyle S[u]=

\displaystyle S[\tilde{u}]=S\bigg[\{{u}(\mathbf{R},\tau)+\sum_{mn}Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]^{mn}\phi_{mn}\}\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle S[u]+\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime},m,n}\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}\phi_{mn}

(S417)

Therefore we have

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime},m,n}\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}\phi_{mn}=0

(S418)

\phi_{mn}
is an infinitesimal arbitrary
4\times 4
traceless Hermitian matrix. Here we focus on the off-diagonal components and have

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime},m,n}\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}=0\quad,m\neq n

(S419)

We next define the functional
C[u]_{mn}

\displaystyle C[u]_{mn}=\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime},m,n}\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}

(S420)

such that Eq.
S419
can be written as

\displaystyle C[u]_{mn}=0,\quad m\neq n

(S421)

Since Eq.
S419
and Eq.
S421
, holds for any given configuration of
\{u(\mathbf{R},\tau)\}
, we must have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta C[u]_{mn}}{\delta u(\mathbf{R},\tau)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}}=0,\quad\text{for all }\mathbf{R},\tau,i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}

(S422)

where
m\neq n
. And consequently

\displaystyle\frac{\delta C[u]_{mn}}{\delta u(\mathbf{R},\tau)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}}\bigg|_{u=0}=0,\quad\text{for all }\mathbf{R},\tau,i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}

(S423)

To evaluate Eq.
S423
, we perform an expansion in
u
. We first consider
Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]
. From Eq.
S414
, we have

\displaystyle Du[u(\mathbf{R},\tau)]_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}^{mn}\approx Du[0]^{mn}_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}+O(u)=\delta_{i,m}\delta_{j,n}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}+o(u)

(S424)

We next perform expansion of
S
. We have already derived the effective action up to the second order
S_{eff}
(Eq.
S390
)

\displaystyle S[u]=S_{eff}[u]+O(u^{3})

(S425)

(for the purpose of discussion,
S_{eff}
is treated as a as a functional of
u
and is written as
S_{eff}[u]
). Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}\approx\frac{\delta S_{eff}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+O(u^{2})

(S426)

Since we have separated
S_{eff}
into diagonal (Eq.
S391
) and off-diagonal part (Eq.
S396
):
S_{eff}=S_{eff,diag}+S_{eff,off}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\delta S[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}\approx\frac{\delta S_{eff,diag}[u]}{\partial u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+\frac{\delta S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+O(u^{2})

(S427)

Combining Eq.
S420
, Eq.
S424
and Eq.
S427
, we find

\displaystyle C[u]_{mn}=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\bigg[\frac{\delta S_{eff,diag}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+\frac{\delta S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+o(u^{2})\bigg]\bigg[\delta_{i,m}\delta_{j,n}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}+o(u)\bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\bigg[\frac{\delta S_{eff,diag}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+\frac{\delta S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}+o(u^{2})\bigg]\bigg[\delta_{i,m}\delta_{j,n}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}+o(u)\bigg]

(S428)

We next consider Eq.
S423
with
i_{2}\xi_{2}\neq j_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta C[u]_{mn}}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i_{2}\xi_{2},j_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}}\bigg|_{u=0},\quad i_{2}\xi_{2}\neq j_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}

(S429)

Combine Eq.
S428
and Eq.
S429

\displaystyle 0=\frac{\delta C[u]_{mn}}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i_{2}\xi_{2},j_{2}\xi_{2}}}\bigg|_{u=0}=\bigg\{\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\bigg[\frac{\delta\frac{\delta S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i_{2}\xi_{2},j_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}}+o(u^{2})\bigg]\bigg[\delta_{i,m}\delta_{j,n}\delta_{\xi,\xi^{\prime}}+o(u)\bigg]\bigg\}_{u=0}

(S430)

since
S_{eff,diag}
(Eq.
S391
only contains the diagonal components of
u
, its contribution vanishes at leading order. Then

\displaystyle 0=\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi}\frac{\delta^{2}S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i\xi_{2},j\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta u_{m\xi,n\xi}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}\bigg|_{u=0},\quad\text{for all $\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2},i\xi_{2},j\xi_{2}^{\prime},m,n$ with $i\xi_{2}\neq j\xi_{2}^{\prime}$ and $m\neq n$}

(S431)

To evaluate Eq.
S431
, we rewrite transform Eq.
S399
into imaginary-time and real space

\displaystyle S_{eff,off}[u]=

\displaystyle\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2}}\sum_{i\xi,i\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{fill},j\xi^{\prime},j\xi_{2}\in S_{emp}}u(\mathbf{R}_{1},\tau)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}\bigg\{\partial_{\tau}\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}\delta_{\mathbf{R}_{1},\mathbf{R}_{2}}-F_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{ij}(\mathbf{R}_{2}-\mathbf{R}_{1})\bigg\}u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau)_{j\xi_{2},i\xi_{2}^{\prime}}

(S432)

where
F_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{ij}(\mathbf{R})=\frac{1}{N_{M}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}}F_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{ij}(\mathbf{q})e^{i\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathbf{R}}
. We take

\displaystyle i\xi_{2}\in S_{fill},j\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{emp},i\neq j

\displaystyle m=j,n=i

(S433)

and then

\displaystyle\frac{\delta S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i\xi_{2},j\xi_{2}^{\prime}}}=\partial_{\tau_{2}}u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{j\xi_{2},i\xi_{2}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{2}}-\sum_{\mathbf{R}_{3}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi_{3}\\
\text{ $j\xi_{3}\in S_{emp}$}\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi_{3}^{\prime}\\
\text{ $i\xi_{3}^{\prime}\in S_{fill}$}\end{subarray}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi_{3}^{\prime}\xi_{3}}(\mathbf{R}_{3}-\mathbf{R}_{2})u(\mathbf{R}_{3},\tau)_{j\xi_{3},i\xi_{3}^{\prime}}\,.

(S434)

(Note that
i,j
are not summed over)
We next take
m=j,n=i
and find

\displaystyle\frac{\delta^{2}S_{eff,off}[u]}{\delta u(\mathbf{R}_{2},\tau_{2})_{i\xi_{2},j\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\delta u_{m\xi,n\xi}(\mathbf{R},\tau)}=\partial_{\tau_{2}}\delta(\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R})\delta(\tau_{2}-\tau)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\\
\text{ $j\xi\in S_{emp},i\xi\in S_{fill}$}\end{subarray}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

(S435)

Combining Eq.
S431
and Eq.
S435
, we find

\displaystyle 0=\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\xi}\sum_{\xi}\bigg[\partial_{\tau_{2}}\delta(\mathbf{R}_{2},\mathbf{R})\delta(\tau_{2}-\tau)-\sum_{j\xi\in S_{emp},i\xi\in S_{fill}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})]\bigg]

\displaystyle=-\int_{\tau}\sum_{\mathbf{R},\xi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\\
\text{ $j\xi\in S_{emp},i\xi\in S_{fill}$}\end{subarray}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R}-\mathbf{R}_{2})

(S436)

which indicates

\displaystyle 0=\sum_{\mathbf{R}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\\
\text{ $j\xi\in S_{emp},i\xi\in S_{fill}$}\end{subarray}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi\xi}(\mathbf{R})

(S437)

(Note that
i,j
are not summed over)
Transforming to the momentum space, we reach the following symmetry constraint on
F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2},\xi\xi}

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\xi\\
\text{ $j\xi\in S_{emp},i\xi\in S_{fill}$}\end{subarray}}F^{ij}_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi\xi}(\mathbf{q}=0)=0

(S438)

with
\xi_{2},\xi_{2}^{\prime}
satisfies
i\xi_{2}\in S_{fill},j\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{emp}
and
i\neq j
. (Eq.
S433
). This constraint will lead to exact Goldstone modes.

S8.9
Symmetry

We next discuss the symmetry of the ground state given in Eq.
S335
and Eq.
S336
.
The effective theory (Eq.
S396
) has the same symmetry properties as the ground state since the action describes the fluctuation on top of the ground state.
At
\nu_{f}=0
, we have
U(2)\times U(2)
. The first
U(2)
symmetry corresponds to the flavors
i=1,2
, where we have filled two
f
-electrons
(\xi=\pm 1)
for each flavor. The second
U(2)
symmetry corresponds to the flavors
i=3,4
, where both flavors are filled with zero
f
electrons. At
\nu_{f}=-2
, we have a
U(1)\times U(3)
symmetry. The
U(1)
symmetry corresponds to the flavor
i=1
, where we have filled two
f
-electrons
(\xi=\pm 1)
. The
U(3)
symmetry comes from the flavors
i=2,3,4
, where all three flavors are filled with zero
f
electrons. At
\nu_{f}=-1
, we have
U(1)\times U(1)\times U(2)
. The first
U(1)
corresponds to the flavor
i=1
, where we have filled two
f
-electrons. The second
U(1)
corresponds to the flavor
i=2
, where we have filled one
f
electron. The
U(2)
corresponds to the flavor
i=3,4
, where we have filled two
f
electrons.

At
\nu_{f}=0,2
, the ground states (defined in Eq.
S335
and Eq.
S336
) also have
C_{3z}
,
C_{2x}
and
C_{2z}T
symmetry. As for
\nu_{f}=-1
, the ground state (defined in Eq.
S335
and Eq.
S336
) only has
C_{3z}
symmetry. This is because, at
\nu_{f}=-1
,
i=2
spin-valley only has one
f
-electrons.

We next define the symmetry transformation of the
f
-electrons and conduction
c
-electrons. To begin with, we introduce the representation matrix
D^{f}(g),D^{c^{\prime}}(g),D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(g)
for a given symmetry operator
g
as following

\displaystyle g\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}g^{-1}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}\psi^{f,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}_{g\mathbf{R},j}D^{f}(g)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}

\displaystyle g\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g^{-1}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}\psi^{c^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}_{g\mathbf{R},j}D^{c^{\prime}}(g)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}\quad,\quad g\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g^{-1}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}\psi^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}_{g\mathbf{R},j}D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(g)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}

(S439)

For the symmetries we considered, the corresponding representation matrices
Song and Bernevig 2022
are

\displaystyle D^{f}(C_{3z})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\xi}\quad,\quad D^{f}(C_{2x})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}\quad,\quad D^{f}(C_{2z}T)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}

\displaystyle D^{c^{\prime}}(C_{3z})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}\xi}\quad,\quad D^{c^{\prime}}(C_{2x})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}\quad,\quad D^{c^{\prime}}(C_{2z}T)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}

\displaystyle D^{c^{\prime}}(C_{3z})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=\delta_{i,j}\delta_{\xi^{\prime},\xi}\quad,\quad D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(C_{2x})_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}\quad,\quad D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(C_{2z}T)_{j\xi^{\prime},i\xi}=[\varsigma^{\prime}_{0}\rho_{z}]_{ji}[\zeta_{x}]_{\xi^{\prime}\xi}

(S440)

where
\zeta_{0,x,y,z}
are identity matrix and Puli matrices for the
\xi
degrees of freedom,
\rho_{0,x,y,z},\varsigma_{0,x,y,z}
are introduced in Eq.
S20
.

In addition, the system at
M=0
also has a flat-
U(4)
symmetry. As introduced in Eq.
S404
, we characterize the flat-
U(4)
transformation with a
4\times 4
traceless Hermitian matrices
\phi_{ij}
. We use
g_{\phi}
to represent the corresponding symmetry operator and introduce the representation matrices as
D^{f}(U(4),\phi),D^{c^{\prime}}(U(4),\phi),D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(U(4),\phi)
. The representation matrices are defined below

\displaystyle g_{v}\psi_{\mathbf{R},i}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}g_{v}=\sum_{j}\psi_{\mathbf{R},j}^{f,\xi,{\dagger}}vD^{f}(U(4),\phi)_{ji}\quad,\quad

\displaystyle g_{v}\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g_{v}=\sum_{j}\psi_{\mathbf{k},j}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}D^{c^{\prime}}(U(4),\phi)_{ji}\quad,\quad g_{v}\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g_{v}=\sum_{j}\psi_{\mathbf{k},j}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(U(4),\phi)_{ji}\quad,\quad

\displaystyle D^{f}(U(4),\phi)=D^{c^{\prime}}(U(4),\phi)=D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(U(4),\phi)=e^{i\phi}

(S441)

where
e^{iv}
denotes the matrix exponential.

We next discuss the effect of symmetry on the single-particle Green’s function. For a given symmetry
g
of
{\hat{H}}_{c,order}
(Eq.
S360
)

\displaystyle g\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g^{-1}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{g\mathbf{k},j}^{c^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}D^{c^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}j^{\prime},\xi i}\quad,\quad g\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi,{\dagger}}g^{-1}=\sum_{j,\xi^{\prime}}\psi_{g\mathbf{k},j}^{c^{\prime\prime},\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}D^{c^{\prime\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}j^{\prime},\xi i}

(S442)

The corresponding Green’s functions (Eq.
S366
) satisfy (for the unitary transformation)

\displaystyle\langle T_{\tau}\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{a,\xi}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},j}^{a^{\prime},\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},0)\rangle_{0}=\sum_{i_{2},\xi_{2},j_{2},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}D^{a,*}(g)_{\xi_{2}i_{2},\xi i}D^{a^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}j,\xi_{2}^{\prime}j_{2}}\langle T_{\tau}\psi_{g\mathbf{k},i_{2}}^{a,\xi,{\dagger}}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},j_{2}}^{a^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}}(g\mathbf{k}^{\prime},0)\rangle_{0}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\sum_{i_{2},\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}D^{a}(g)^{*}_{\xi_{2}i_{2},\xi i}D^{a^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}i,\xi_{2}^{\prime}i_{2}}g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi^{\prime}_{2},i_{2}}(g\mathbf{k},\tau)

(S443)

where
a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}
and we use the fact that
\langle T_{\tau}\psi_{\mathbf{k},i}^{a,\xi}(\tau)\psi_{\mathbf{k},j}^{a,\xi^{\prime},{\dagger}}(0)\rangle=0
when
i\neq j
(because
{\hat{H}}_{c,order}
is blocked diagonal with respect to the flavor indices
i
as shown in Eq.
S360
).
Then the product of two Green’s functions satisfy (for the unitary transformation)

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})=\sum_{i_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{3}^{\prime}}\sum_{j_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{4}^{\prime}}D^{a}(g)_{\xi_{3}i_{2},\xi i}^{*}D^{a^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}i,\xi_{3}^{\prime}i_{2}}D^{a_{2}}(g)_{\xi_{4}j_{2},\xi_{2}j}^{*}D^{a_{2}^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}j,\xi_{4}^{\prime}j_{2}}g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},i_{2}}(g\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}^{\xi_{3}\xi_{3}^{\prime},j_{2}}(g\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})

(S444)

For anti-unitary transformation, we have

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=\sum_{i_{2},\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}_{2}}D^{a}(g)^{*}_{\xi_{2}i_{2},\xi i}D^{a^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}i,\xi_{2}^{\prime}i_{2}}\bigg(g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi^{\prime}_{2},i_{2}}(g\mathbf{k},\tau)\bigg)^{*}

\displaystyle\ g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})=\sum_{i_{2},\xi_{3},\xi_{3}^{\prime}}\sum_{j_{2},\xi_{4},\xi_{4}^{\prime}}D^{a}(g)_{\xi_{3}i_{2},\xi i}^{*}D^{a^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi^{\prime}i,\xi_{3}^{\prime}i_{2}}D^{a_{2}}(g)_{\xi_{4}j_{2},\xi_{2}j}^{*}D^{a_{2}^{\prime}}(g)_{\xi_{2}^{\prime}j,\xi_{4}^{\prime}j_{2}}

\displaystyle\bigg(g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},i_{2}}(g\mathbf{k},\tau)\bigg)^{*}\bigg(g_{a_{2}a_{2}^{\prime}}^{\xi_{3}\xi_{3}^{\prime},j_{2}}(g\mathbf{k}^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})\bigg)^{*}

(S445)

S8.10
\nu_{f}=0

We now analyze the effective theory at
\nu_{f}=0
and
\nu_{f}=-2
.

At
\nu_{f}=0
, we consider the
U(2)\times U(2)\in U(4)
symmetry
g_{\phi}
(in Eq.
S441
,note that
U(2)\times U(2)
is a subgroup of flat
U(4)
). From Eq.
S443
.

\displaystyle[e^{i\phi}]_{ij}g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)[e^{-i\phi}]_{ji}=g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)

(S446)

For the ground state we considered in Eq.
S335
, one
U(2)
corresponding to the rotations of flavor
i=1,2
and the other
U(2)
corresponding to the rotations of flavor
i=3,4
. Thus
e^{i\phi}
is block diagonal

\displaystyle[e^{i\phi}]_{ij}=\begin{bmatrix}e^{i\phi_{1}}&0_{2\times 2}\\
0_{2\times 2}&e^{i\phi_{2}}\end{bmatrix}_{ij}

(S447)

where
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}
are two traceless Hermitian matrices that characterize two
U(2)
symmetries. Therefore, Eq.
S446
can only be satisfied when

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)\quad,\quad a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}\quad,\quad i,j\in\{1,2\}

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)\quad,\quad a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}\quad,\quad i,j\in\{3,4\}

(S448)

Consequently, from Eq.
S397
and Eq.
S448
, we have

\displaystyle\chi_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{A}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i,j)=\chi_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{A}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i^{\prime},j^{\prime})\quad,\quad i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}\quad,\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\{3,4\}

\displaystyle N_{i\xi}-N_{j\xi^{\prime}}=N_{i^{\prime}\xi}-N_{j^{\prime}\xi^{\prime}}\quad,\quad i,i^{\prime}\in\{1,2\}\quad,\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\{3,4\}

(S449)

We can thus define (Eq.
S398
)

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=F_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{ij}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\},j\in\{3,4\}

(S450)

which does not depend on
i,j
. Then the effective action in Eq.
S399
can be written as

\displaystyle S_{eff,off}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi N_{M}}\int\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\sum_{i\xi,i\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{fill},j\xi^{\prime},j\xi_{2}\in S_{emp}}u(\mathbf{q},i\omega)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}u(-\mathbf{q},-i\omega)_{j\xi_{2},i\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\bigg\{i\omega\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}-H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}\bigg\}d\omega

(S451)

We then discuss the discrete symmetries. We first illustrate the symmetry properties of single-particle Green’s function. We consider
C_{2z}T
,
C_{2x}
and
C_{3z}
. From Eq.
S440
, Eq.
S443
and Eq.
S445
, we have

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=(g_{aa^{\prime}}^{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{2z}T\mathbf{k},\tau))^{*}\quad,\quad a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{aa^{\prime}}^{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{2x}\mathbf{k},\tau)\quad,\quad a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}

\displaystyle g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{3z}\mathbf{k},\tau)e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi)}\quad,\quad g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{3z}\mathbf{k},\tau)

\displaystyle g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{3z}\mathbf{k},\tau)e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(-\xi)}\quad,\quad g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(C_{3z}\mathbf{k},\tau)e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime})}\,.

(S452)

Thus using Eq.
S397
, Eq.
S451
and Eq.
S452
, we find

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(H^{\nu_{f}}(C_{2z}T\mathbf{q})_{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}})^{*}

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=H^{\nu_{f}=0}(C_{2x}\mathbf{q})_{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=H^{\nu_{f}=0}(C_{3z}\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

(S453)

In addition, from Eq.
S400
and Eq.
S450
indicates
{\hat{H}}^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})
is a Hermitian matrix

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=[H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\xi}]^{*}

(S454)

We next consider the long-wavelength limit (or small
\mathbf{q}
expansion) of
H^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
. We let

\displaystyle M^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}\approx C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+\sum_{\mu=\{x,y\}}C_{\mu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}q_{\mu}+\sum_{\mu,\nu\in\{x,y\}}C_{\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}q_{\mu}q_{\nu}

(S455)

where
C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}},C_{\mu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}},C_{\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
are the coefficients of the expansion. We next combine Eq.
S454
, Eq.
S454
and Eq.
S455
and derive the symmetry constraints of the coefficients.
For
C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
, we find

\displaystyle C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(C_{0,\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=C_{0,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

\displaystyle C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{0,\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\xi}

(S456)

Thus, we can introduce three real numbers
C_{0,0},C_{0,1},C_{0,2}
:

\displaystyle C_{0,0}=C_{0,++,++}=C_{0,--,--}\quad,\quad C_{0,1}=C_{0,+-,+-}=C_{0,-+,-+}

\displaystyle C_{0,2}=C_{0,++,--}=C_{0,--,++}

(S457)

and all other components of
C_{0,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
are zero.

As for
C_{\mu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
, we find (using Eq.
S454
, Eq.
S454
and Eq.
S455
)

\displaystyle C_{\mu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{\mu,\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}}^{*}

\displaystyle C_{x,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{x,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}\quad,\quad C_{y,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=-C_{y,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle C_{x,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(-\frac{1}{2}C_{x,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}

\displaystyle C_{y,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}})e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}\quad,\quad C_{y,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(-\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}C_{x,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-\frac{1}{2}C_{y,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}})e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

\displaystyle C_{\mu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=-C_{\mu,\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\xi}

(S458)

Then we can introduce a real number
C_{1}

\displaystyle C_{1,1}=

\displaystyle C_{x,++,-+}=C_{x,--,+-}=C_{x,-+,++}=C_{x,+-,--}=-C_{x,+-,++}=-C_{x,-+,--}=-C_{x,++,+-}=-C_{x,--,-+}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle iC_{y,++,-+}=-iC_{y,--,+-}=-iC_{y,-+,++}=iC_{y,+-,--}=-iC_{y,+-,++}=iC_{y,-+,--}=iC_{y,++,+-}=-iC_{y,--,-+}

(S459)

As for the
C_{\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
, Eq.
S454
, Eq.
S454
and Eq.
S455
we have

\displaystyle C_{\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(C_{\mu\nu,\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2},\xi\xi^{\prime}})^{*}

\displaystyle C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{xx,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}\quad,\quad C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=C_{yy,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=-C_{xy,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}\quad,\quad C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=-C_{yx,-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=\frac{C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+3C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-\sqrt{3}C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-\sqrt{3}C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}}{4}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

\displaystyle C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=\frac{3C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+\sqrt{3}C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+\sqrt{3}C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}}{4}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

\displaystyle C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=\frac{\sqrt{3}C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-\sqrt{3}C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-3C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}}{4}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

\displaystyle C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=\frac{\sqrt{3}C_{xx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-\sqrt{3}C_{yy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}-3C_{xy,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}+C_{yx,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}}{4}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

(S460)

We can then introduce the following real numbers

\displaystyle C_{2,0}=C_{xx,++,++}=C_{yy,++,++}=C_{xx,--,--}=C_{yy,--,--}

\displaystyle C_{2,1}=C_{xx,+-,+-}=C_{yy,+-,+-}=C_{xx,-+,-+}=C_{yy,-+,-+}

\displaystyle C_{2,2}=C_{xx,++,--}=C_{yy,++,--}=C_{xx,--,++}=C_{yy,--,++}

\displaystyle C_{2,3}=C_{xx,+-,-+}=-C_{yy,+-,-+}=-iC_{xy,+-,-+}=-iC_{yx,+-,-+}=C_{xx,-+,+-}=-C_{yy,-+,+-}

\displaystyle=iC_{xy,+-,-+}=iC_{yx,-+,+-}

(S461)

and all other components of
C_{\mu\nu,\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}
vanishes.

In summary, we have the following
q
expansion of
H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})=\begin{bmatrix}C_{0,0}+C_{2,0}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{0,2}+C_{2,2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(q_{x}-iq_{y})\\
C_{0,2}+C_{2,2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{0,0}+C_{2,0}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{1,1}(q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}+iq_{y})\\
C_{1,1}(-q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{0,1}+C_{2,1}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{2,3}(q_{x}^{2}-q_{y}^{2}-2iq_{x}q_{y})\\
C_{1,1}(q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{2,3}(q_{x}^{2}-q_{y}^{2}+2iq_{x}q_{y})&C_{0,1}+C_{2,1}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}\end{bmatrix}

(S462)

Finally, we utilize the symmetry constraints introduced in Eq.
S438
. We take
i,j=(1,3)
(or equivalently
(i,j)=(1,4),(2,4),(2,3)
) and
\xi_{2}=\xi_{2}^{\prime}=++
in Eq.
S438
and find

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)_{++,++}+H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)_{++,--}=0

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle C_{0,0}+C_{0,2}=0

(S463)

Then we have
C_{0,0}=-C_{0,2}
and

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})=\begin{bmatrix}C_{0,0}+C_{2,0}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&-C_{0,0}+C_{2,2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(q_{x}-iq_{y})\\
-C_{0,0}+C_{2,2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{0,0}+C_{2,0}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{1,1}(q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}+iq_{y})\\
C_{1,1}(-q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{0,1}+C_{2,1}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&C_{2,3}(q_{x}^{2}-q_{y}^{2}-2iq_{x}q_{y})\\
C_{1,1}(q_{x}+iq_{y})&C_{1,1}(-q_{x}-iq_{y})&C_{2,3}(q_{x}^{2}-q_{y}^{2}+2iq_{x}q_{y})&C_{0,1}+C_{2,1}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}\end{bmatrix}

(S464)

where we note that
C_{0,0}=-C_{0,2}
leads to a Goldstone mode at
\mathbf{q}=0
.

We also provide the expression of the parameters

\displaystyle C_{0,0}=[H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{++,++}

\displaystyle C_{2,0}=[(\partial_{q_{x}})^{2}H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{++,++}

\displaystyle C_{2,2}=[(\partial_{q_{x}})^{2}H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{++,--}

\displaystyle C_{1,1}=-[(\partial_{q_{x}})^{2}H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{++,+-}

\displaystyle C_{0,1}=[H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{+-,+-}

\displaystyle C_{2,1}=[\partial_{q_{x}}^{2}H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{+-,+-}

\displaystyle C_{2,3}=[\partial_{q_{x}}^{2}H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)]_{+-,-+}\,.

(S465)

In practice, we first calculate
N_{i\xi}
,
\chi^{A}
and
H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})
(Eq.
S397
, Eq.
S455
) and then find the values of
C_{0,0},C_{2,0},C_{2,2},C_{1,1},C_{0,1},C_{2,1},C_{2,3}
using Eq.
S465
. The values of parameters are discussed in Sec.
S8.12
.

We now discuss the number of Goldstone modes. The original model has (flat)
U(4)
symmetry with rank (number of independent generators)
16
. At
\nu_{f}=0
, the symmetry group of the ground state (Eq.
S335
) is
U(2)\times U(2)
with rank
4+4=8
. Then the number of Goldstone modes is
(16-8)/2=4
, where
1/2
comes from the fact that each Goldstone mode is a complex boson
Bernevig et al. 2021c
. We find
M^{\nu_{f}=0}_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})
has one zero eigenvalues at
\mathbf{q}=0
, which corresponds to the Goldstone mode. However, since we have four ways to pick
i,j
indices (of the bosnoic fields
u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}
for the given ground state in Eq.
S335
), with
(i,j)=(1,3),(2,3),(1,4),(2,4)
, we have one Goldstone mode for each
i,j
choices and
4
Goldstone modes in total.

In the spectrum of spin excitation, we also observe a quasi-degeneracy between gapped modes at
\Gamma_{M}
at
\nu=0
. At
\mathbf{q}=0
, we note (Eq.
S464
)

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q}=0)=\begin{bmatrix}C_{0,0}&-C_{0,0}&0&0\\
-C_{0,0}&C_{0,0}&0&0\\
0&0&C_{0,1}&0\\
0&0&0&C_{0,1}\end{bmatrix}

(S466)

Four eigenstates are
0,2C_{0,0},C_{0,1},C_{0,1}
. Then there is one Goldstone zero mode and three gapped modes. We next estimate the energy difference between the gapped modes

\displaystyle\Delta E=

\displaystyle C_{0,1}-2C_{0,0}=\frac{1}{4}\bigg[\chi_{\xi\xi,\xi\xi}(\mathbf{q}=0,i\omega=0,i,j)-\chi_{\xi\xi,-\xi-\xi}(\mathbf{q}=0,i\omega=0,i,j)-\chi_{\xi-\xi,\xi-\xi}(\mathbf{q}=0,i\omega=0,i,j)\bigg]

\displaystyle i\in\{1,2\},j\in\{3,4\}

(S467)

Approximately, we take the non-interacting single-particle Green’s functions (given in Sec.
S9
) to calculate
\Delta E
. From Eq.
S397
, we have From Eq.
S388
, we find

\displaystyle\Delta E\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\bigg\{\frac{2\gamma^{4}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}\bigg[2g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)-g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi-\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)-g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{-\xi-\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]

\displaystyle+2J^{2}\bigg[2g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}^{\xi\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]-\frac{2J\gamma^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\bigg[2g_{c^{\prime\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g^{\xi\xi,i}_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\frac{2\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}}{D_{\nu_{f},\nu_{c}}^{2}}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\bigg[-2g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g^{\xi\xi,i}_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)-2g_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi,i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)g^{\xi\xi,j}_{c^{\prime}c^{\prime}}(\mathbf{k},-\tau)\bigg]\bigg\}d\tau

(S468)

Using non-interacting single-particle Green’s function (Eq.
S366
, Eq.
S548
, Eq.
S557
, Eq.
S558
), we find

\displaystyle\Delta E\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\mathbf{k}}\frac{1}{N_{M}}\bigg[4J^{2}\frac{2}{4}\frac{1}{2|v_{\star}||\mathbf{k}|}+\frac{4J\gamma^{2}e^{-|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\frac{2}{4}\frac{1}{2|v_{\star}|\mathbf{k}|}-\frac{8\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}e^{-2|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}e^{-2|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\lambda^{2}}|\mathbf{k}|^{2}\frac{2}{4}\frac{1}{2|v_{\star}|\mathbf{k}|}\bigg]

(S469)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4A_{MBZ}}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{0}^{\Lambda_{c}}\bigg(\frac{J^{2}}{|v_{\star}|}+\frac{J\gamma^{2}e^{-k^{2}\lambda^{2}}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}|v_{\star}|}-\frac{2\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}e^{-2k^{2}\lambda^{2}}k^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}|v_{\star}|}\bigg)dkd\theta

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{\pi}{2A_{MBZ}|v_{\star}|}\bigg[J^{2}\Lambda_{c}+\frac{J\gamma^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c,\nu_{f}}}}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}Erf[\lambda\Lambda_{c}]}{2\lambda}-\frac{2\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}}\frac{1}{16\lambda^{3}}\bigg(-4\lambda\Lambda_{c}e^{-2\lambda^{2}\Lambda_{c}^{2}}+\sqrt{2\pi}Erf[\sqrt{2}\Lambda_{c}\lambda]\bigg)\bigg]

(S470)

where
Erf[x]
is the error function.
Approximately, we take the momentum cutoff
\Lambda_{c}=1/\lambda
and find the energy difference between two gapped modes is

\displaystyle\Delta E=\frac{\pi}{2A_{MBZ}|v_{\star}|\lambda}\bigg[J^{2}+\frac{0.747J\gamma^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}-\frac{0.231\gamma^{2}(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}}{D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}^{2}\lambda^{2}}\bigg]

(S471)

where
\lambda=0.3375a_{M}
is the damping factor of the hybridization term.
The degenerate condition
\Delta E=0
is

\displaystyle\alpha=\frac{\gamma^{2}}{JD_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}}\bigg[-0.374+\sqrt{0.140+0.231\frac{(v_{\star}^{\prime})^{2}}{\gamma^{2}\lambda^{2}}}\bigg]=1

(S472)

Using the realistic values of each parameter, we find
\alpha=1.074\approx 1
.

S8.11
\nu_{f}=-2

At
\nu_{f}=-2
,
we have
U(1)\times U(3)
symmetry.
U(1)
acts on the flavor
i=1
and
U(3)
acts on the flavor
i=2,3,4
. Thus Green’s function in flavor
i=2,3,4
are equivalent

\displaystyle g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},i}(\mathbf{k},\tau)=g_{aa^{\prime}}^{\xi\xi^{\prime},j}(\mathbf{k},\tau)\quad,\quad a,a^{\prime}\in\{c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\}\quad,\quad i,j\in\{2,3,4\}

(S473)

Consequently, from Eq.
S397
and Eq.
S473
, we have

\displaystyle\chi_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{A}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i=1,j)=\chi_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{A}(\mathbf{q},i\omega=0,i=1,j^{\prime})\quad,\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\{2,3,4\}

\displaystyle N_{1\xi}-N_{j\xi^{\prime}}=N_{1\xi}-N_{j^{\prime}\xi^{\prime}}\quad,\quad j,j^{\prime}\in\{2,3,4\}

(S474)

We can thus define (Eq.
S398
)

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=F_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}^{ij}(\mathbf{q})

\displaystyle i=1,j\in\{2,3,4\}

(S475)

which utilize the symmetry properties of Eq.
S474
.
Then the effective action in Eq.
S396
can be written as

\displaystyle S_{eff,off}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\pi N_{M}}\int\sum_{\mathbf{q}}\sum_{i\xi,i\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{fill},j\xi^{\prime},j\xi_{2}\in S_{emp}}u(\mathbf{q},i\omega)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}u(-\mathbf{q},-i\omega)_{j\xi_{2},i\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\bigg\{i\omega\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}-H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}\bigg\}d\omega

(S476)

We next consider the discrete symmetry. We have
C_{3z},C_{2z}T,C_{2x}
. Using Eq.
S452
, we find

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=(H^{\nu_{f}}(C_{2z}T\mathbf{q})_{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}})^{*}

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(C_{2x}\mathbf{q})_{-\xi-\xi^{\prime},-\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}=H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(C_{3z}\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}e^{i\frac{2\pi}{3}(\xi^{\prime}-\xi_{2}+\xi_{2}^{\prime}-\xi)}

(S477)

In addition, the definition of
H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}
(Eq.
S450
) and Eq.
S400
, we have

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=[H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi^{\prime}\xi}]^{*}

(S478)

Finally, we utilize the symmetry constraints in Eq.
S438
.
We take
i,j=(1,2)
(or equivalently
(i,j)=(1,2),(1,4)
) and
\xi_{2}=\xi_{2}^{\prime}=++
in Eq.
S438
and find

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q}=0)_{++,++}+H^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q}=0)_{++,--}=0

(S479)

Combining Eq.
S453
, Eq.
S477
and Eq.
S479
), we conclude
{\hat{H}}^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})
follows the same constraint under symmetry as
{\hat{H}}^{\nu_{f}=0}(\mathbf{q})
(Eq.
S453
, Eq.
S454
, Eq.
S463
).
Therefore, the long-wavelength behavior of
{\hat{H}}^{\nu_{f}=-2}(\mathbf{q})
also takes the form of Eq.
S464
.

We now discuss the number of Goldstone modes. At
\nu_{f}=-2
, the symmetry group of the ground state (Eq.
S335
) is
U(1)\times U(3)
with rank
1+9=10
.
The symmetry of the original Hamiltonian at
M=0
has flat
U(4)
symmetry with rank
16
.
The number of Goldstone modes is
(16-10)/2=3

Bernevig et al. 2021c
. From Eq.
S464
,
H^{\nu_{f}=-2}_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}\xi_{2}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{q})
has one zero eigenvalues at
\mathbf{q}=0
, which corresponds to the Goldstone mode. Since we have three ways to pick
i,j
indices (of the bosonic fields
u_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}
), with
(i,j)=(1,2),(1,3),(1,4)
(for the given ground state in Eq.
S335
), we have one Goldstone mode for each
i,j
choices and
3
Goldstone modes in total.

We also comment that, due to the fact that values of
\gamma^{2}/D_{\nu_{c},\nu_{f}}
at
\nu_{f}=2
is much larger than its value at
\nu_{f}=0,-1
. The Kondo interaction is much stronger at
\nu_{f}=-2
, which leads to a larger bandwidth of the excitation spectrum.

S8.12
Lagrangian at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
in the long-wavelength limit

In this section, we provide the Lagrangian of effective theory at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
in a more compact form. From Eq.
S451
, Eq
S476
and Eq.
S464
, we have the following Lagrangian density in the long-wavelength limit at
\nu_{f}=0,-2

\displaystyle L=\sum_{i\xi,i\xi_{2}^{\prime}\in S_{fill},j\xi^{\prime},j\xi_{2}\in S_{emp}}u(\mathbf{q},i\omega)_{i\xi,j\xi^{\prime}}u(-\mathbf{q},-i\omega)_{j\xi_{2},i\xi_{2}^{\prime}}\bigg\{i\omega\delta_{\xi_{2},\xi^{\prime}}\delta_{\xi_{2}^{\prime},\xi}-H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}\bigg\}

(S480)

where we introduce a conventional parameterization of
H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}

\displaystyle H^{\nu_{f}}(\mathbf{q})_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2}+(\frac{1}{4m_{0}}+\frac{1}{4m_{1}})|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&-\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2}+(\frac{1}{4m_{0}}-\frac{1}{4m_{1}})|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{+}&-\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{-}\\
\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2}+(\frac{1}{4m_{0}}-\frac{1}{4m_{1}})|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&\frac{\Delta_{1}}{2}+(\frac{1}{4m_{0}}-\frac{1}{4m_{1}})|\mathbf{q}|^{2}&-\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{+}&\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{-}\\
\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{-}&-\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{-}&\Delta_{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{2m_{2}}&\frac{q_{-}^{2}}{2m_{3}}\\
-\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{+}&\frac{V}{\sqrt{2}}q_{+}&\frac{q_{+}^{2}}{2m_{3}}&\Delta_{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{2m_{2}}\end{bmatrix}_{\xi\xi^{\prime},\xi_{2}^{\prime}\xi_{2}}

(S481)

where the row and column indices are
(++,--,+-,-+)
,
q_{\pm}=q_{x}\pm iq_{y}
. The parameters are

\displaystyle\Delta_{1}=2C_{0,0},\quad\Delta_{2}=C_{0,1}

\displaystyle m_{0}=\frac{1}{2(C_{2,0}+C_{2,2})},\quad m_{1}=\frac{1}{2(C_{2,0}-C_{2,2})},\quad m_{2}=\frac{1}{2C_{2,1}},\quad m_{3}=\frac{1}{2C_{2,3}}

\displaystyle V=-\sqrt{2}C_{1,1}

(S482)

We then take the following new basis

\displaystyle u_{j,i}(\mathbf{q},i\omega)=(u_{(j+,i+)}(\mathbf{q},\omega)+u_{(j-,i-)}(\mathbf{q},i\omega))/\sqrt{2}

\displaystyle U_{j,i}^{T}(\mathbf{q},i\omega)=((u_{(j+,i+)}(\mathbf{q},i\omega)-u_{(j-,i-)})(\mathbf{q},i\omega)/\sqrt{2},u_{(j+,i-)}(\mathbf{q},i\omega),u_{(j-,i+)}(\mathbf{q},i\omega))

(S483)

The Lagrangian (Eq.
S480
) can then be written as

\displaystyle L=L_{Goldstone}+L_{gapped},

\displaystyle L_{Goldstone}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\xi\in{S}_{fill}\\
i\xi\in{S}_{emp}\end{subarray}}u_{j,i}^{\dagger}({\bf q},i\omega)\Big(i\omega-q^{2}/2m_{0}\Big)u_{j,i}({\bf q},i\omega),

\displaystyle L_{gapped}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j\xi\in{S}_{fill}\\
i\xi\in{S}_{emp}\end{subarray}}U_{j,i}^{\dagger}({\bf q},i\omega)[i\omega\hat{I}-H(\mathbf{q})]U_{j,i}({\bf q},i\omega),

(S484)

where

\displaystyle H(\mathbf{q})=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}\frac{q_{+}q_{-}}{2m_{1}}+\Delta_{1}&Vq_{+}&-Vq_{-}\\
Vq_{-}&\frac{q_{+}q_{-}}{2m_{2}}+\Delta_{2}&\frac{q_{-}^{2}}{2m_{3}}\\
-Vq_{+}&\frac{q_{+}^{2}}{2m_{3}}&\frac{q_{+}q_{-}}{2m_{2}}+\Delta_{2}\end{array}\right)

(S489)

and
\hat{I}
is a
4\times 4
identity matrix. After transforming from Matsubara frequency to real frequency (
i\omega\rightarrow\omega
), we reach the same formula given in the main text (Eq.[11] and Eq.[12]).

We also provide the dispersion of gapped modes. By diagonalizing
H(\mathbf{q})
in Eq.
S489
, we find the dispersion of three gapped modes are

\displaystyle E^{gap,1}_{\mathbf{q}}=\Delta_{2}+(\frac{1}{2m_{2}}+\frac{1}{2m_{3}})|\mathbf{q}|^{2}

\displaystyle E^{gap,2}_{\mathbf{q}}=\frac{\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}}{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{4}\bigg[\frac{1}{m_{1}}+\frac{1}{m_{2}}-\frac{1}{m_{3}}\bigg]+\sqrt{2V^{2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}+\bigg[\frac{\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}}{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{4}\bigg[\frac{1}{m_{1}}-\frac{1}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{m_{3}}\bigg]\bigg]^{2}}

\displaystyle E^{gap,3}_{\mathbf{q}}-\frac{\Delta_{1}+\Delta_{2}}{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{4}\bigg[\frac{1}{m_{1}}+\frac{1}{m_{2}}-\frac{1}{m_{3}}\bigg]-\sqrt{2V^{2}|\mathbf{q}|^{2}+\bigg[\frac{\Delta_{1}-\Delta_{2}}{2}+\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{4}\bigg[\frac{1}{m_{1}}-\frac{1}{m_{2}}+\frac{1}{m_{3}}\bigg]\bigg]^{2}}

(S490)

We provide the numerical values of parameters (derived from Eq.
S465
and Eq.
S482
) at
\nu_{f}=0,-2
in Tab.
S1
. In Fig.
S5
, we also compare the dispersion from the effective model (Eq.
S489
) with the dispersion from directly evaluating Eq.
S399
. We note that the effective model
S489
correctly predicts the long wavelength (small
\mathbf{k}
) behaviors but failed to capture the large momentum behaviors such as the roton modes. To recover the roton mode, we need to keep high-order contribution (
\mathbf{q}^{3}
term).
